Nanya *pantha* vidyate *ayanayah*. 14. Five types of conscience. 15. The supreme query. 16. Human life and cosmic ideation. 17. Spiritual Practice, clash and cohesion. 18. Sadhana and attraction for the supreme. 19. Force and application of force. 20. Ascension of mind. 21. The devotee and his Lord. 22. Tantra and Sadhana. 23. The cognitive faculty and psychic elevation. 24. *Karmasanyasa* and Parabhakti. 25. The cult of devotion. 26. The ideation of *Brahma*. 27. Omni-active and Cognitive faculty. 28. Sadhana and Madhuvidya. 29. Mantra caetanya.

Serialised publications of *Subhasita Samgraha* (17 parts) were printed between 1966 and 1990. The books have been included in the syllabus of comparative religion in some universities in the world.

**Ananda Vacanamrtam (32 Parts)**—Wherever *Marga Guru* goes a large number of devotees would congregate. Reverend Marga Guru would meet them and grace them with important intellectual and spiritual discourse. All these discourses are meaningful and suited to the occasion. All the recorded speeches were carefully preserved and published in the series of Ananda *Vacanamrta*. Once, reverend Marga Guru gave a series of discourses on Vaedika Gods and Goddess, Tantrika *Deities*, *Baodha*, and the Laokika deities. Those discourses are printed in "Ananda Vacanamrtam" 11th part. Likewise, the Marga Guru, in response to an earnest request of a devotee, delivered a series of talks on *Krsna*. Each of those was fascinating. Later those were published in "*Ananda Vacanamrta*" (17th part). All the 32 parts are highly educative and ennobling.

**Tattvika Kaomudii (3 Parts)**—The Reverend *Marga Guru* has explained the basic ideas of moral code, intuitional science and spiritual philosophy, sometimes from very high altitude of intellectuality. Sometimes He would interpret with proper commentaries and annotations, both lucid and revealing. He would expound his brilliant idea on mind, sensory and motor organs, nerves, intellect, plexus, glands, hormones, cerebral and extra-cerebral memory, acquired knowledge and self knowledge, individual rhythm and universal rhythm, etc. Subsequently, they were printed and published in the series of *Tattvakaomudi*.

**Ananda Marga Caryacarya (3 Parts)**—The vastness of *Marga Guru's* literature is divided into three main divisions: *Dharma Shastra*, *Darshan Shastra* and *Samaja Shastra*. The scope of *Samaja Shastra* is vast. *Ananda Marga Caryacarya* (all the parts) is included in *Samaja Shastra*. According to *Marga Guru*, *Dharma* is collective affair as it is an individual affair. The structure of a society rests on the solid foundations of morality and spirituality. Furthermore, just as Q and U are closely related, similarly morality and spirituality are also deeply interdependent. So to run the social structure smoothly, the social observance and ceremonies must have a direct corelation with morality and spirituality. From the viewpoint, the *Marga Guru* has written His social treaties (3 parts). The first part deals with: First feeding of food and naming of child, laying a foundation stone, first entry into a new house, planting tress, starting on a journey, the marriage system, birth day ceremony, social functions and festival, *Dharmacakra*, *Tattva*

Sabha, Jagrti, disposal of dead bodies, Shradha ceremony, dress, livelihood, economic policy, livelihood of women, social punishment, the widow, science and society, the ideal house holder, self analysis, your various organisations, Acarya board, Tattvika board, *Purodha* board etc. Ananda Marga Caryacarya part two deals with *Sadhana*, health, society, different vacations, 15 Shiilas, some rules and regulations, 16 points etc. Part-III deals with system of bath, food, fresh air, physical restraint, yogic exercises, *asana*, mudra, *bandha*, and *pranayama* etc.

Prout (18 Parts)—While explaining the main thesis of Ananda *Marga* ideology, *Marga* Guru said that for an ideal society, six factors are indispensable: Spiritual philosophy, spiritual cult, social outlook, socio-economic-political theory, scriptural philosophy and social code, and guide preceptor. True to His conviction, the Marga Guru has been propounding His ideas on economics, politics, sociology, history and allied topics since 1959, which in due course became to known as PROUT, an acronym for progressive utilization theory. All His dictations and discourses on PROUT have been serially published in the name of PROUT in a Nutshell (21 parts). In this series of publications, the author has dealt with the evolution of society, morality, education, justice, social justice and judicial system, criminal psychology, social psychology, decentralization of industries, industrial policy, mechanization of industries, trade union movement, cooperative movement in agriculture, industry and commerce, dowry system, war and peace, casteism, provincialism, world script and world confederation, social cycle, culture and civilization, social value and cardinal value, dialecting materialism and democracy, "Isms" and human progress, theory and practice on social reconstruction, the theory of selfish pleasure and social equality, art of service and blessedness, block level and inter-block planning, population growth and control, socio-economic groupification and movement, women's rights, democracy and functionalism, compartmentalization of democracy, balanced economy, and modes of agricultural production. Needless to say, Shrii *P.R.Sarkar's* PROUT theory has been attracting intellectuals both at home and aboard.

Literature—In the evolution of human society, the value of literature is immeasurable. Literature provides food for human mind - a brain tonic. Just as bad food brings physical disease, similarly vulgar literature pollutes the human mind and degrades human thinking. That is why for people to roam in the world of aesthetics, the mere collection of ideas and command over language is not suffice. One must possess a sympathetic heart and thinking mind saturated with penetrating intellect. Sahitya or literature has two distinct roles to play. First, it has to provide pure joy to the readers, and secondly, it must ensure well being to the society at large. Everything in this world is on the move - it is moving along the divine flow of melody, rhythm, form, color, and smell. Sahitya or literature makes this path of movement rhythmic and dynamic with sweetness and aesthetic beauty, it rather makes the path with a flow of bliss. Furthermore, it is not enough that literature should have the capacity of providing joy to its readers. It must also possess the inherent capacity to have the welfare of its readers. It must be imbued with the inner zeal of ensuring maximum good to the society. Shrii *P.R.Sarkar* is very clear on the side of literature when He articulates, arts for service and blessedness. It literally means that all the flowers of aesthetics should be like a blooming sunflower, facing towards the supreme blissful entity. Human society is moving on and on in a cyclical order from one era to another. The end of one era culminates in the birth of another era. Human society offers a dismal look today. Art, culture, civilization, spirituality, science, moral values all are in

a state of abject decadence. It seems all the leaves of the tree of society have turned pale and yellow. They will have to fall off sooner or later. At this critical moment of transition, the rising poets, literateurs, and artists will have to shoulder the responsibility of bringing about new blossoms onto the tender leaves and the more one is adept in that, the more successful one will be as a writer. Shrii *P. R. Sarkar's* first literature heralds the dawn of a new era in His all-embracing philosophy, in each stage of His scheme of action, in tune of His composition, in His presentation of His ideas and languages used, in all His articulation in style of expressions. The message of a new age is conspicuously manifest. The range of *P. R. Sarkar's* literature is vast.

Stories—The Prabhat *Sahitya* marks a new dimension to the literary stories. There is considerable difference between stories and essays and novels not only in size but also in quality. The composition of an essay is somewhat light at times, difficult for readers to digest. The fictions and novels are somewhat lengthy, the composition is a bit loose but it provides enough scope to convey many things, but the stories are simple, straight, and short in size. And within the limited scope, the writer has to record the event or events as well as the problems and the hints of solutions thereof unambiguously. That is why the appeal of short stories in the field of literature is quite different.

All the stories have been published in the series of Sarkar's short stories. It is not possible here to discuss in detail about the characteristics of His stories.

### Liberation of Intellect (Neo-Humanism)

"The greatest human treasure is their intellect. What can be more painful if we cannot make the best use of this intellect, Hence, the importance of the Liberation of mind?" Prior to that there must be liberation of human intellect, it must be free from all sorts of bondages, from the shackles of dogma, from the various inauspicious influences. Until that is done, the future of human race cannot be golden. If we wish to walk in a golden door to humanity of today, then we have to wage a war against dogma and bring about the total liberation of human intellect. Now a question arises, what is dogma? The author explains dogma, saying, "The most dangerous thing for human society as well as its progress is dogma. What is dogma? Where there is no logic, where there is no support of intellect or where there is no debate or discussion, but when there is forced imposition, where people are ordered to obey some mandate without a murmur - this is called dogma". The author, in this book under review, while explaining Neo-Humanism, has explained in detail about "*Atmasukha Tattva*" (The principle of selfish pleasure), dogma, psycho-economic exploitation, social, political, economic, and cultural exploitation, geo-sentiment, socio-sentiment, proto-spiritualistic mind, demons in human form, metamorphosed sentimental strategy and counter-strategy, socio-sentiment minimitis, socio-sentiment excellence, internationalism, pseudo-humanism, spirituality as a cult, spirituality as a mission, etc. Finally, He has extolled the virtues of Neo-humanism for the all-round welfare of human society.

The human race has entered the threshold of a new era. At this time, we cannot afford to waste our valuable time by any means. We must make the maximum utilization of all the inherent capacities of the human race. The universe does not belong to humans only, it belongs to all. This age is an age of Neo-Humanism and with the cooperation of all we have to build up a neo-humanistic social structure. As we have said, humanity has entered a new era. In this age, various epoch-making events are about to take place. We must bear in mind that in human society, the age of dogma is over.... Human society is

one and indivisible. What we want today is the all-round progress of human society, and together with the human progress, we simultaneously want to do the progress of the living and non-living world. Thus, what is needed today is the all round elevation of human existence, i.e. their physical, psychic, and spiritual progress. We want to bid farewell to dogma forever. We want greater rationality, which will lead the humanity towards the supreme entity. Only this type of Neo-Humanism can save humanity. The author, in this book, has sung the homilies to Neo-Humanism of this type.

### Stratification of Psycho-Spiritual Progress

Knowledge is of two types - mundane knowledge and supermundane knowledge. The realized persons - Munis and Rs'is, although they considered the mundane knowledge necessary, did not attach supreme importance to it, rather, they considered spiritual knowledge as absolute. According to them -

> "A'tmajina'nm vidurjina'narn Jina'n'a'nyani ya'ni tu
> Ta'ni jina'na'v'abha'sa'ni sa'rasya naeva bodhana't"

The internal knowledge - self knowledge, the knowledge of self and not of mind is the real knowledge. Human beings, in fact, cannot attain anything thereby. However learned or pedantic one might claim to be, he or she is only groping the darkness of objective knowledge. They cannot get the real knowledge. For subjectivization of objectivities, the humans depend on perception, inference, and authority. But these three means of subjectivisation are not totally reliable. The author has pointed out the imperfections of these three sources of knowledge, and on the other hand, He has made an in-depth study of acquiring the real knowledge. The whole process does not concern the relative world. This is out and out of a psycho-spiritual affair. Hence the author, by means of His penetrating intellect and profound intuition has tried to explain in a very simple and lucid way, some of the secret psycho-spiritual phenomena. Incidentally, He delivered fourteen discourses on different strata of the psycho-spiritual sphere. (i.e. *Yatmana*, *Vyatireka*, *Ekendriya*, and *Vashiika'ra*). Those important discourses have been published in the name of "Stratification in Psycho-Spiritual Practice". This book is of immense value to the seekers of spiritual knowledge.

### Varna Vijianana

Varna Vijianana or science of letter. Here, Varna means letters. This four hundred and twenty one-page book is complete and inimitable book on comparative philosophy. This book contains valuable information on the style and system of pronunciation, and Sanskrit oriented languages. Besides that, many other useful chapters on spelling, syntax and phonetics have also been added. The chapters on specialties of language, differences between languages and dialect will be, in my opinion highly useful for students, teachers, and research scholars on philology. In this book style of language, derivation, emanation, and distortion of the words have been discussed in detail. In short, this book is almost like a valuable mine of linguistic information for students and teachers of language and literature.

The Reverend Marga Guru, since the very inception of the organization in 1955, used to discuss grammar and philology, but these were written down years later, in 1983, when the camp headquarters of the organization were transferred to Calcutta, He started His marathon series of discourses on grammar and philology during Sunday general darshana. The series of Calcutta discourses continued from June 1983 to November 6th, 1983. These Sunday discourses were later compiled and published in the name of *Varna Vijianan*.

### Laghu Nirukta

In the Vedic age, Nirukta meant a dictionary with difficult Vedic words. The author has written *Laghu Nirukta*, a light dictionary. Many are not fully aware of

the general and particular meanings, the derivatives of the words. Consequently, while giving of their inner thoughts, they use many words which are often incorrect. For instance, many persons use the word *Sambhranta* in the sense of *S'abhijata* (aristocratic), *Aparyapta* (sufficient), *Soccara* or *Mukhara* (vocal), *Mahashankha* in the sense of *brhatshankha* (big conch shell). In fact, *sambhranta* means one that has made a big mistake in life. *Soccara* means one who has passed stool, but has not cleaned oneself. *Mahashankha* does not mean a big conch shell; it means a skull. In this six hundred page voluminous book, the author has dealt with over six thousand important words with derivations, general and particular compendium meanings. In high studies and researches on Bengali philology, this is almost indispensable.

### Bengala and Bengali

This book is a collection of matters taken from so many books. The Bengali as a distinct race on this earth has specialties in their society, culture, and civilization. And these distinctive specialties they gain as a result of Aryan, Mongol, and Austric civilizations, born by the rivers—the Ganges, the Brahmaputra, and those of the Ancient Rarh. These distinctive Bengali specialties have been reflected in the social structure and religious beliefs, their literature, agriculture and industry, their architecture and culture, and even in their political ideals. The author, in His five hundred page book, has mentioned many important historical facts, past and present. Some of the topics discussed are as follows: Place in Tantra and Indo-Aryan civilization, Rarh—the starting point of civilization (in this part, the author has thrown light on Rarh's population, animals, Language, Literature, and culture, Rarh's script, Mangol Kavya and Vaishnava Kavya and Vaei'hava Ka'vya, Rarh's temple, natural wealth and climate, etc.), Gondwanaland, Ethnicity, River valley civilization, tidbits of history, new year and spring festival cultural relation between Bengal and Magadha, Mithila, Manipur, Arunachal, Bhutan, Nepal and Kerala. A brief outline: social history, religious beliefs, agriculture and industry, contemporary thinkers, etc. In a nutshell, those interested in the socio-economic-cultural history of Bengal will find this book interesting and useful.

### Yogika Treatment and Dravyaguna

The purpose of medical treatment is to provide physical and psychic relief to patients. Hence it is immaterial to uphold the prestige and dignity of a particular school of medicine as the main thing is to alleviate the suffering of the patients. Just as a diseased body can be restored to normalcy by administering medicine externally or internally, similarly, it is quite possible to restore the natural state of health through Yogic exercises in a much safer and better way. The purpose of this book is to acquaint the public with the system of treatment of major diseases.

Let the people be free from diseases by practicing *Asanas* and *Mudras* as mentioned in this book. This is the inner desire of the author. It is better that the reader should, in order to minimize the risk, first consult competent teachers of yogic exercises. With the *Asanas* and *Mudras* there is also reference to some medicines inexpensive or less expensive that have been found highly effective. The system of their use has also been mentioned. Some of the diseases discussed in the book are dyspepsia, hernia, acidity, piles, cancer, dysentery, syphilis, gall bladder stone, diabetes, elephantiasis, hydrocele, etc.

### The Mahabharata

The *Mahabharata* is one of the four best epics in the history of the world. The influence of this single book on the Indian population over the centuries has been phenomenal. Each of the characters of the *Mahabharata* is bold and lively. Directly or indirectly, they offer to teach human beings in some way or other. The Reverend Marga Guru, during His stay in Ranchi, gave a series of illuminating

discourses on the Mahabharata. The significance of the title of the book: Sri Krs'n'a—the hero of the epic, system of education, system medical treatment, social structure, standard of morality of the *Mahabharata* age. Lord *Krs'n'a's* style of functioning some prominent characters, *Bhiisma*, *Drona*, Gandharii, *Vidura*, *Kuntii*, *Draopadii*, Evaluation of Karna's character, Krs'n'a the grand planner of the Great Indian war, Krs'n'a the nucleus of the universe, *Mahasambhuti* Sri Krs'n'a, and the plan for the great universe.

**Varna Vicitra (8 Parts)**—Since the 13th November, 1983 the Reverend Marga Guru has been writing books on philology and grammar. He started to discuss each and every letter of the Indo-Aryan alphabet, and the series of discourses continued till 1st September, 1985. The discourses were later published in the name of *Varn'a Vicitra* in 8 parts, running over 2000 pages. The author has analysed the role, pronunciation, genesis, and meaning of the concerning letter. He has also shown how the letter has found its place in the original (*tatsama*) and derivative word (*tadbhava*) as well as in indigenous and exogenous vocabularies. He has further pointed out the role of the co-occurring letter in Sandhi (joining of words), *Upasarga* (prefixed), and *biija mantra*. Because of the variety of uses of the letters in words, the series has been named *Varn'a Vicitra*. This book is dealing with the letters.

**Shabda Cayanika (26 Parts)**—Since September 8, 1985, the Reverend Marga Guru started another new series. According to the author one essential precondition for recognition of a language is its own vocabulary (the other conditions being 1. own pronouns, 2. own verb-ending, and 3. own case endings, 4. literature, 5. intonation, 6. psycho-acoustic notes, 7. inferential acoustic notes, 8. and syntax with the growth of human intellect, the vocabulary became enriched. For instance, where there is enormous development in science, like physics, chemistry, biology, botany, mining, engineering, aeronautics, nuclear physics, the great scientists and professors would keep coining new words to express their subtle ideas. The same thing applies to humanities, medicine, art, architecture, scripture, aesthetics, etc. Now, behind the creation of any word lays its etymology, its literal meaning and special meaning. For instance, Buddha used to say "Attahi *atta'nam nath*". Now if we want someone to be intellectually strong, we will have to introduce them to etymology and proper use of the term Atta. As per the rules of metamorphosis in *Magadhi* Pra'krta, particularly in Eastern demi *Ma'gadhii*, *Attma* becomes Atta. The author, the great linguist, had dealt with a large number of such words alphabetically. In some cases, the discussion on one single word continued for 20-30 pages. The author conducts the series of discourses for five years from the 8th September, 1985 to the 14th October, 1990. The series is of encyclopedic nature. In over 6000 pages, the author has primarily discussed philology and grammar, secondarily various other branches of human knowledge.

**Music**—Songs, dances and instrumental music and all these together constitute music. Songs are apparently related to the physical world, but the vibration affects the inner mind. In music, just as there is an idea there is rhythm and melody also. Instrumental music is not centered on an idea. The purpose of instrumental music is to vibrate the ectoplasmic stuff by vibrating the mind and maintaining coordination with idea. The purpose of dance is to articulate the inner idea with the help of rhythm and Mudra (body postures). The occidental dances are primarily *mudraic*, but it does take the help of rhythm. The author has also discussed an aesthetic science and supra aesthetic science in this book on music.

Talks on Ideal Farming (2 Parts)—We have already stated that the author had unbounded interest in all subjects of learning. Whenever any topic came before Him for discussion, He took pains to elaborate on it before people. Hence nothing was excluded from the purview of His cultivation of knowledge. The author guided people to increase the quantum of products through scientific farming. His talks on farming have been published in the series of "Talks on Ideal Farming" in 2 parts.

'Abhimata (8 Parts)—It has been mentioned earlier that the author was a gigantic intellectual and a man of profound insight. With His unbounded erudition and profound wisdom, He could delve deep into many problems of society, state, morality, history, aesthetics, art, literature, language, archeology, etc., and offer His considered views. Those comments and views have been published under the name of "Abhimat" (A few problems solved) 8 parts. Each of the part is a valuable source of information.

Birds and Animals (Our good neighbors)—To Shrii P.R. Sarkar, the great neo-humanist, human beings are the very dignified children of the Lord. Nonetheless, the lives of animals and plants are not less important. So the developed human beings have a great responsibility and duty towards animals and birds, their good neighbors. It does not add to their greatness if humans follow the dictum, "The living are the food of the living" or "might is right." That is why every little of the book suggests the neo-humanistic outlook of the author.

Prabhata Samgiita (5018 Songs)—Prabhat Samgiita, a grand total of 5,018 songs marks the beginning of a new chapter in the world of music. On the 14th September, 1982, the Reverend Marga Guru composed the first song of Prabhat *Samgiita* and wrote the last song of Prabhat Samgiita, a song of the proposed Ananda Marga University. In the points of inner import, language, variety of melody and rhythm, Prabhat *Samgiita* is brilliant with distinctive specialities. This new school of songs contains various types of songs, for instance, devotional songs, mystic songs, songs on social ceremonies and observance, patriotic songs, folk songs, like *Jhumur*, *Baul*, etc., Kiirtan, *Gazal*, Qawwali, songs on Shiva, Krsna, etc. The author Himself is both lyricist and melodist. He composed songs in 8 languages, Sanskrta, Bengali, Angika, Hindi, Urdu, Maethili, Magahii, and English. The songs have been published in 20 parts.

Prabhata Ranjaner Vyakaran Vijanana (93 parts)—This is a rare series in three parts on comparative philology and grammar. There is a vast difference between this series and the other books on grammar and philology available in the market. In the earlier chapter of the first part, the author has thrown searching light on phonetics of Bengali, Samskrta and foreign languages. Remaining chapters deal with letters from A to GHA. Their role and uses in different words and sentences have been explained in broad detail.

The second part deals with the introduction of ten new letters. A very useful chapter on suffixes and prefixes has also been added. The third important chapter contains the list of errors, errors due to ignorance of etymology of words, anarchy in spelling, errors due to ignorance regarding the use of "Ba and Va", as well as numerous errors in a variety of ways in over 200 pages.

The third part deals with the origin and development of language, difference between language and dialect, the case endings, the formation of words in naming of villages and towns, Samasa, evolution, emanation and distortion of words, etc. The greatest attraction of the series in the three hundred

pages chapter on root verbs (*Sanskrit*) and the vocabulary. The role of root verbs and suffixes in the derivation of *Sanskrit* oriental languages is undeniable. Even highly well placed people are found ignorant of the meanings of their own names. The main reason is the total ignorance of the knowledge of root verbs, suffixes, and prefixes. Besides this, the author has incidentally discussed "*Adii* and *Antasha La*", *Virgiita Ba*, and *Antastha Va*, the rules regarding the exact uses of *N'a*, *Na*, short '*i*', long '*i*' or long '*II*', long *lrr*, etc.

# V

## MY MASTER: HIS ORGANISATION

> “You all move together, radiate one and the same thought-wave and form a universal mind with all unit minds.
>
> You shall unitedly enjoy all the properties of the universe in the same manner as the sages, in olden times, used to accept the havih (food of a *yajina*).
>
> You shall have a common ideal and all of you shall be inseparable from one another.
>
> Infuse in your minds one common sentiment whereby you may be well united.”

> [Non-text image or illustration present; no text transcribed.]

### Organisation as a Mission

Society is like a company of pedestrians going on a pilgrimage, suppose one among them is stricken by cholera. Do the rest of them go on their way leaving him behind? No they can't. Rather, they break their journey at the place for a day or two to relieve him from the disease and help him to acquire strength in his legs or they start out anew carrying him on their shoulders. If anyone runs short of his subsistence, others give him their own. Together, they stream ahead, singing their leading chorus in their eagerness to go ahead with others, they forget their trifling differences, which in their families might have led to negative exchanges, in some cases, even for several generations.

The essence of co-operation born of moving together arises at the widening or expanding of the mental being of a person by striking down one's barriers of meanness again and again. Hence I reiterate that society is like such a batch of pilgrims gathering a strange power of mind in traveling together and with its help, solving all the problems of their individual and social life.

My Master (*P.R. Sarkar*) "Life is an art - He designed it"

Planning and blueprint of the organisation - *Vardhaman* is a very old township of the land of Rarh. *Bamunpara* is a locality nearby. My master's father, the late *Laksmi Narayan Sarkar*, was born there. The *Sarkar* family had lived in *Rarh* for generations. This old and influential family practiced both *Tantrika* and *yogic* techniques and many of its members were developed deeply in the path of spirituality. *Laksmi Narayan* left the ancestral home and eventually came to *Jamalpur* in *Bihar* State as railway employee. My master, *Shrii Shrii*

Anandamurti was born at Jamalpur.

When my master was seven years old, He went, with His elder sister Hiraprabha, to Bamunpara during summer vacation to the ancestor's home. At that His parents became very concerned about His future and they even contemplated removing Him from the environment of Jamalpur, because they could not understand the phenomena surrounding Him. For instance, one day, while at Bamunpara, my master was lying on His cot, His sister started scolding him saying, "At your age, boys are learning many things, whereas you pass your time all day lying on your cot. What do you think of while lying there? You have not even learnt Bengali well yet." But my master, Prabhat, was not a boy to be subdued. That same morning, He sat and in no time, has written His name in Bengali, Arabic, Devanagari, *Telagu*, Tamil, and Latin. His sister was astonished to see this and she never again scolded Him. Later, my master told devotees, those days when I was lying in bed in the hut in *Bamunpara*, I was making the blueprint of the organization. Whatever you see today in the organization throughout the world is the yield of my thought process at the age of seven.

He mentioned once that when He was six years old was seriously thinking about world problems and their solution. When He turned twelve, He was quite clear about what He had to do. When He was in age eighteen, He started to work in an isolated way to start this mission. He added that He had to wait until after the Second World War so that the collective psychology of the people in the world could be changed.

**Preparations for the organisation** - My master, *Prabhat Ranjan*, was a student at *Keshopur* primary school from 1927 to 1930. He started high school in 1937 and in 1939 He passed His high school examinations with distinction. He then joined the *Vidyasagar* College in Calcutta. During this time, He started working. Then *Kalicharan* (first initiates) was given His spiritual name *Kalikananda* Avadhuta. My master returned to *Jamalpur* in 1941 and joined the railway department as an accountant. At that time in Jamalpur, people had developed different opinions about my master, some were saying that He was an astrologer; some said He was a great palmist while others said He was an intellectual. Other opinion expressed was that He was an honest moralist, a great spiritualist, a humanist, a philosopher, and even a doctor. He was the focal point at His work place.

With His accumulated intellect and spiritual power, He started giving initiation in Jamalpur. He first taught Yama and Niyama (the moral principles of Ananda Marga) and then followed initiation.

During 1954, the initiates were not known to each other and they were ordered to meditate alone and secretly. Initiation was given only by my master and there were only about one hundred initiates, mainly from Jamalpur railway workshop and surrounding areas of the district.

**Physical form of the organisation** - The Second World War had come to an end. This forest fire of a war had not only burnt the peaceful abode of the human race but had simultaneously increased unrest, mutual suspicion and distrust a hundred fold. Showers of bullets and bombs had destroyed the integrity of humanity and the peoples love for virtue. Humanity gradually moved towards animality and away from the true spirit of the society.

At this critical stage of history, at this critical moment of civilization, a few sought ways to save human society from total erosion. How could it be saved from international fighting and killing, which brings nothing but shame to humanity?

In the midst of turmoil life of a human being my master, Shrii Shrii Anandamurtii stated clearly about drawbacks and showed the solution. Moral erosion is a mental disease. As physical ailments can be cured by medicine, so mental disease

too can be cured by medicine. The way to purify the heart is not through book knowledge, drugs, medicine or a plethora of theories. This is obvious to a practical person. Instead of talk, what is needed is the cultivation of a scientific spiritual practice. My master, Anandamuritijii commenced His work to inspire people to practice spirituality in order to establish them in morality and spiritual strength.

My master called His disciples and discussed with them and sought their suggestions and opinions as to what name should be given to the new spiritual movement. Many devotees proposed many different names. One name was "Pusti Marga" which means the path which is spiritually nourishing. Baba, with a frown, listened intently, watching the sincerity of His disciples. The seventh of November 1954 is a memorable day in the history of Ananda Marga. On this day at 338 Rampur Railway colonies, Baba delivered His first address to a gathering of His disciples. At that gathering, Baba declared for the first time, your goal is Brahma. Since attainment of Brahma was the goal, the name of the organization became 'Brahma *Ista Marga*' (literally, the path of those whose goal is Brahma). Later, on the day of the full moon in the month of Shravanii, the organization was given a new name: "Ananda Marga Pracaraka *Samgha*" literally, the organization that propagates the path of bliss).

On the 14th of November, 1959, at the same place and with a greater number of Sadhakas (attending the meeting), Baba disclosed, to some extent, the mysteries of life and death. On December 25, 1959, my master directed His disciples to prepare the bylaws and the articles. Accordingly, Pranaya Kumar *Chatterjee*, Chandranath Kunwar Sukumar Bose *Nagina Prasad Singh*, *Shishir Dutta*, and other prominent disciples drew up a draft constitution for the proposed organization. Finally then, my master amended the draft and gave His approval. Many disciples from all over got the news by word of mouth that a new organization would be formed on January 1, 1955. At this meeting, the appointment of President, General Secretary and other members of the Central Committee were finalized.

At 7 o'clock, Marga Guru came to 338, Ranipur Railway colony, the temporary headquarters of the Marga. He gave a discourse entitled the gradual evolution of society. On January **9th**, a conference was held at Olipur area of *Jamalpur*. All the members of the Central Committee of the Marga were introduced to everyone. The members of the central committee:

* President - Prabhat Ranjan Sarkar (My Master)
* General Secretary - Pranaya Kumar Chatterjee
* Finance Secretary - ...
* Publication Secretary - ...
* Members - 1..., 2..., 3...

**Dharma Cakra (D.C - Collective Meditation)** - After the *Anand Marga* Society was founded it began to take on an organizational shape. Every Sunday the great Sadhakas meditated collectively, sitting in rows and lines and then participated in the collective meetings that followed. Both men and women attended sitting in separate rows. Before meditation they chanted three times two couplets from Suktika 182, **10th** mantra (chapter) of Rgveda, which is over 16,000 year old (What is this mantra? - see the chapter Dharma Cakra in second part.)

**Dharma Maha Cakra** - When the different Sadhakas of different places assembled for a few days and participated in collective spiritual programs of devotional songs, *Kiirtan* and collective Meditation with *Marga Guru* present, it was known as *Dharma Maha Cakra* (DMC). In the DMC *Marga Guru* gave the general discourse and *Barabhaya Mudra* (special

gesture of blessing at the end of the discourse.)

Dharma Maha Sammelan - In cases of DMC when Marga Guru is not physically present and any other is representing Him are called *Dharma Maha Sammelan*. Even when Guru is present but not giving the special Barabhaya Mudra this also is called Dharma Maha Sammelan.

The family Acarya (spiritual teacher) - My master, *Anandamurtijii* gave initiations up to 6 February 1955 Himself. The organization however, was expanding very fast. He therefore made family Acaryas in the organization. The first group of these Acaryas was created from a class held from 15th to 18th in March 1955. Later, they also graduated as *Tattvikas* and they were declared on May 5th in Ananda Purnima DMC at Monghyr. They included Ac Pranaya *Kumar*, Ac Chandranath, Ac Shishira *kumar*, Ac Aruna *kumar* and Ac *Shacinandan*.

Whole Timer Workers - At first there were no whole timers in the organization. In 1961, Baba summoned up sixteen margiis from different provinces and held a meeting. He wanted a state wide Pracar (propagation) program. Baba allotted duties to them and gave them one month to decide whether they would or would not do it. After the month had passed all of them except one, gave different excuses for their inability to do it. Their reasons included failure to obtain disheartened children, and so on. Obviously, there was a need for full time workers who would not be bothered with commitments such as these.

As an alternative, Baba asked "Will you give me permission to create monks and nuns to take these responsibilities?" The margiis nodded together. In this way, Baba started system of monks and nuns in Ananda Marga, and He initiated the first monk on May on 17, 1962.

Global organization - Amurt - Hong Kong, Amurtel - Berlin sector (Italy, Rome), Amupreso - NY Sector (Philadelphia), RU: Cairo Sector (Istanbul Turkey), E.M.S - Nairobi Sector (Ghana), PCAP & CL - Sydney Sector (Sydney), AMSAI - Manila Sector (Medan), AMJAS: NY Sector (Los Altos), AMPS - George Town Sector (Mendoza)

Structure of Ananda Marga Pracarak Samgha- The Ananda Marga organization has been divided into two parts: Structural side and organizational side.

On the structural side, the world is divided into nine sectors, Sectors into Regions, Regions into Diocese, Diocese into Dit, Dit into Blocks, Blocks into Panchayats, Panchayats into Villages and accordingly secretaries are posted in the different levels (Sectorial Secretary, Regional Secretary etc)

On the Organizational side, Ananda Marga is divided into departments, Sections and Branches, from Sectorial level to Village level, and similarly, different Secretaries and in charges are posted (Sectorial ERAWS Secretary, Regional AMURT Secretary, Diocese RAWA Secretary, etc.)

| Sector | Region | Diocese | Dit | Blocks |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 1. Delhi | 10 | 104 | 435 | 5596 |
| 2. Hong Kong | 10 | 68 | 1249 | 6861 |
| 3. Manila | 07 | 62 | 375 | 3003 |
| 4. Suva | 08 | 24 | 60 | 179 |

### Department, Section and Branches

#### ERAWS-
1. Education, a. AMSAI (AM Special Academic (EI - I, EI - II, EI - III) Institution), b. AMJAS (AM *Jagrti* School), c. AMPS AM People's School).
2. Relief R - I, R-II, a. AMURT- Ananda Marga Universal Relief Team, R-III, b. AMURTEL (AM Universal Relief Team Ladies), AMUPRESO (AM Universal Permanent Relief Society).
3. Medical a. PCAP & CL (Prevention of Cruelty to Animals and Plants & Cheap Literature).
4. TBPW (Tribal a. EMS - *Ek Manava Samaja* And Backward People's Welfare).

**Formation of Board-** All the departments and sections of AMPS have boards (from central level to village level) to materialize their respective programs. The total number of AMPS committees should be the same as the uppermost level (central committee).

Some of the boards attached to the lowermost level which may not be required to render any direct service to that particular level may help its sister board in the next upper level or other upper levels. The minimum number of members in a board shall be three and the maximum seven. The members may/may not be educated persons but must have developed a sense of responsibility and no one person can be a member of more than one board. Following is the list of boards attached to a single committee of AMPS.

1. *Dharma Pracar*, 2. Education, 3. AMSAI, 4. AMJAS, 5. AMPES, 6. Relief, 7. AMURT, 8. Publication, 9. AMUPRESO, 10. RAWA, 11. RU, 12. Commerce, 13. AMYS, 14. Finance, 15. Farm, 16. FARPRO, 17. Land, 18. Social Security, 19. Construction, 20. Industry, 21. INPRO, 22. Food and Care, 23. Press and Printing, 24. Medical, 25. PCAP & CL, 26. TBPW, 27. EMS, 28. Society Building, 29. Women's, 30. GV, 31. HPMG (L), 32. PWSA, 33. AMURTEL, 34. *Jagrti*, and 35. PRS.

In AMPS there are 18 departments collectively called AMPS General and one department called ERAWS—Education, Relief and Welfare Section. The departments in AMPS General are - 1. Commerce, 2. Construction, 3. *Dharma Pracar*, 4. Farm, 5. Finance, 6. Food and Care, 7. Industry, 8. ISMUB, 9. *Jagrti*, 10. Land, 11. Master Unit, 12. Press and

Printing, 13. Publication, 14. Public Relations, 15. Renaissance Universal and RAWA, 16. Society Building, 17. Social Security, 18. Women's Welfare.

**Acarya, Tattvika, Purodha and concerned Board** - The *Acarya*, *Tattvika* and *Purodha* trainees will first take lessons from suitably capable persons and then appear at the examination conducted by five other persons (*Acarya*, *Tattvika* or *Purodha* as the case may be). Depending on the results of the examination, the central *Acarya*, *Tattvika* or *Purodha* Board will confer a certificate on the person concerned. The certificate will bear the registration numbers and also the signature of the Examiners. *Tattvika*, *Acarya*, *Purodha*, and *Dharmamitram* are words symbolic of ability and activity. Therefore, if for any reason except old age and deformity, any *Tattvika*, *Acarya* or *Purodha* be unable to discharge their respective duties, the power to cancel their certificates will be with the central *Tattvika* Board, The Central *Acarya* Board, and the Central *Purodha* Board, respectively. An appeal for reconsideration against the decision of the central *Tattvika* board can be made to the Central *Acarya* Board, and in that case, opinion will have to be accepted, accordingly, an appeal for reconsideration against the decision of the Central *Acarya* Board can be made to the Central *Purodha* Board and in that event, the opinion of the latter board will be taken to be finally binding. *Tattvika*, *Acarya*, *Purodha* and such other words will be used as symbol of individual ability. These will have nothing to do with heredity.

In the event of any particular complicated problem or serious controversy in *Ananda Marga*, the decision of the central *Purodha* Board will be final. The unanimous decision of the members of the central *Purodha* board shall be binding on the society. If there is no unanimity among the members of the board, the decision of the majority will be the board's decision. If the two groups are equal in votes, the single vote of the chairman shall be the decision of the board. Each and every *Ananda Margi* shall have to obey the decision of the *Purodha* Board without any argument. The Chairman of the *Purodha* Board will be designated as the *Purodha Pramukha*. The decision of the *Purodha Pramukha* will be considered correct and final. No one else can change the decision of the *Purodha Pramukha*. If he so likes, he can change his decision. The *Purodha Pramukha* will grace his post for his whole life. But, he can resign from the post if he deems it necessary due to physical illness, etc. *Purodha Pramukha* will be elected by the votes of Purodhas. Three members of the *Purodha* Board besides four others will be elected by *Purodhas*. The period of service will be five years. If before completion of five years, anyone dies or is compelled to resign due to illness, the post will be filled by re-election. If the majority of voting *Purodhas* express dissatisfaction with the work of any members of the *Purodha* Board, dependent on the consent of the *Purodha Pramukha*, re-election can be held for this post.

**AVADHUTA and AVADHUTIKA Board** - If it is not possible for anyone to discharge his family responsibilities because of excessive preoccupation with *Dharma* propagation and social service, and if such person takes formal initiation in *Sannyasa*, he/she will be called an *Avadhuta*/*Avadhutika* subject to the approval of the *Purodha Pramukha*. The *Avadhut* Board will be constituted of four persons elected by the *Avadhutas*/*Avadhutikas*, from amongst the elected members; one will act as the secretary of this Board. This board will determine all rules and regulations, punishments and discipline, and everything else regarding *Avadhuta*/*Avadhutikas*. The decisions by this board will be submitted to the *Purodha Pramukha* for final approval. The *Avadhuta*/*Avadhutikas* will obey the *Purodha Pramukha*, and

without his approval, the Board will not enforce any decision.

Acarya Board - Subject to the approval of the Purodha Pramukha, eight persons elected by the *Acarya* will constitute the Acarya Board. One from amongst three elected members will be the secretary. This board will determine all rules and regulations, punishments and discipline, and everything else regarding Acaryas. The decisions by this board are submitted to the Purodha Pramukha for final approval.

Tattvika Board - Subject to the approval of the Purodha Pramukha, twelve persons elected by the Tattvikas will constitute the Tattvikas Board. One from amongst the elected members will be the secretary. This board will determine all rules and regulations, punishments and discipline, and everything else regarding Tattvikas. The decisions by this board will be submitted to the Purodha Pramukha for final approval.

ERAWS Department - The ERAWS department is composed of five sections with various branches. 1. Medical, 2. PCAP/CL - Prevention of Cruelty to Animals and Plants / Cheap Literature, 3. Education - This section has three branches called EI, EI2, and EI 3, which administrate the education section, control the curriculum and supervise all the schools. AMSAI (Ananda Marga Special Academic Institution), AMJAS (Ananda Marga Jagrti Schools) for indigent, AMPES (Ananda Marga Peoples Schools) - Teaching literacy to adults, 4. Relief - This section is composed of three branches RI, R2 and R 3, AMURT [Ananda Marga Universal Relief Team], AMUPRESO (Ananda Marga Universal Permanent Relief Society), 5. TPW - Tribal People's Welfare.

AMPS Departments in detail:

1. Commerce Department - This department supervises the establishment and maintenance of all businesses operating on behalf and for the welfare of the entire organization. The Sectorial Secretary will approve all policy matters and major decisions. The Commerce Department will select and train qualified persons to manage and run the business. Due to local laws regarding business, legal board of directors may be to be set up. The ultimate authority will rest in the hands of the Commerce Department. The Commerce department also supervises AMUS, The *Ananda Marga* Universal Store. The function of AMUS is to purchase essential commodities from the producers at wholesale rates while selling to the general public at reasonable rates. AMUS will run on a no-profit, no loss basis providing maximum service to the public.

2. Construction Department is responsible for providing and maintaining adequate housing facilities for all departments of *Ananda Marga*. In addition, it is the work of Construction Department to build roads, bridges, water supply, and other basic constructions necessary for the establishment of the organization. Financing for such projects will be arranged in coordination with sectorial finance committee and approved by SS.

3. Dharma Pracar Department will supervise the implementation of the central *Dharma Pracar* policies as well as maintain regular communication with all units of *Ananda Marga* within the sector. The main work of Dharma Pracar is:
a. To establish a close knit communications system within the sector.
b. To create and implement new ideas in propagation of *Dharmic* ideals through classes, slide shows, OSC, DDC, (Door to Door Contact) literature and leaflets, magazines etc.
c. Maintain the discipline and conduct of the whole timers and

*Margiis* as per *Yama* and *Niyama* and conduct rules.
d. To encourage the formation of AMPS units with *Jagrities* and to ensure that proper decorum and system of management of *Jagrities* is maintained.
e. To collect the unit registrations form and fee from every unit and to renew the same annually.
f. To keep an updated list of all units and offices of *Ananda Marga* as well as an address list of all Margiis within the sectors.

4. Farm Department will work in co-ordination with the Master Unit Department to develop agriculture, gardening, sericulture, bee-keeping, animal husbandry, dairy farming, plant and soil protection, irrigation, conservation and ecology. The Farm department will also foster a good relationship with all groups both public and private who are related to the above activities. A monthly newsletter should be published to keep the Margiis and public update about the latest trends and ideas in farming.

FARPRO (Farm Products) is a section of the Farm Department. The function of FARPRO is to activate all cultivatable lands of *Ananda Marga* in order to meet the total requirements of staple food for all homes and institutions of *Ananda Marga* in the same district. Secondly, after all requirements are met, to produce cash crops and farm products necessary for the financial maintenance and expansion of the farms.

5. Finance Department will maintain accounts, budgeting and inspection of financial systems within the sector. A sectorial finance committee will advise the SS on all financial matters and prioritize all major projects within the sector. It will be the main objective of the finance department to encourage and assist each unit to become self dependent economically.

6. Food and Care Department is to concentrate on solving the problems of food and proper care of all beings in society:
a. All children and other AM institution members, b. All WTes and LFTes, Old and invalid moralists, c. All moralists, d. All human beings (moral) or anti social, e. Old and invalid animals, f. Decaying species of fauna and flora, g. All animals and plants.

Secondly, the food and care department is to raise public awareness regarding the world food problem, promote food for all movements and publish literature relating to these critical issues.

7. Industry Department will setup industries to help make *Ananda Marga* economically self dependent. i.e. health food, health care, construction, metal shops, electrical appliances, carpentry, weaving, hosiery, tailoring, pottery, sporting goods, school supplies, etc. The INPRO (Industrial Products section) in the industry department that markets the products. The Industry department will work in close cooperation with the Commerce Department in the marketing and business systems and practices employed.

8. ISMUB Department means Inspection, Seminar, Movement, Utilization and Board. Shrii P.R. Sarkar has said that system without speed is useless and speed without system is dangerous. This department is responsible for seeing that the organization is efficiently running according to ideological standards, both in system and in speed.

The Inspection system insures a tight check on whether or not all offices are operating according to standard. Seminar duties are delegated during RDS where in every district of the sector is covered by a specific date, place, organizers and trainer. The seminar topics are given every six months at the time of DMS in *Ananda Nagar*. These topics reflects the current philosophical knowledge being disseminated world wide by *Ananda Marga*. This insures a 'systematic development of collective psychology. Each Margii is

expected to attend the seminar and a specific duty. The seminar reports are to be collected and scrutinized by the ISMUB department.

Movement means the conscious effort by each *Margii* and Whole Timer to see that a dynamic flow is being generated in every unit. For example, food for all movement, employment to all, anti-smoking movement, prevention of cruelty to animals and plants, anti pornography, vegetarian movement, aid to poor students, anti-pollution, etc. These movements throughout the sector are supervised and controlled by the ISMUB department.

Utilization means that a conscious effort is being made by all *Margiis* and whole timers to utilize the physical, mental, and spiritual capacities within *Ananda Marga*. Every land, every building, every objects, every plants, every mind, and every soul should be engaged to the maximum for the upliftment of creation. ISMUB department will be actively supporting this activity. Board means the setting up of advisory committees and working boards (ABC) of every department and on every level, i.e. unit, village, *panchayat*, *upabhukti*, diocese, region, and sector.

This will insure the proper management and supervision of the entire organization. It is the duty of the ISMUB department to monitor and control the efficiency of the boards and committees.

9. The Jagrti Department supervises the construction and maintenance of all *Ananda Marga* Jagrties. While working in coordination with the construction department for planning and design and *Dharma Pracar* Department for discipline and maintenance. This department will be responsible to upgrade the standing of *Ananda Marga* throughout the sector by providing a proper living environment for workers and *Margiis*.

10. Land Department will be responsible for the legal work necessary for procuring lands for the organization. Where legally possible, all land should be registered in the name of *Ananda Marga*. No one has the right to sell any property of the organization without permission from the Central land secretary. The land department should keep proper records of all lands owned by *Ananda Marga*.

11. The Master Unit Department supervises the use of land allocated for Master Units. As the name implies, all the departments of *Ananda Marga* are given scope to express themselves on the Master Unit. Many research activities as well are slated to develop on the Master Unit, especially in the field of agriculture. The ultimate aim is to create a self-sufficient economic complex, which provides a holistic environment for all round development.

12. Press and Printing department is responsible for establishing and maintaining presses and related equipment of *Ananda Marga*, and for training personnel to operate them properly. Once a press is established it should be commercialized so that becomes economically self-sufficient. Press equipment is always owned by *Ananda Marga* and should never be sold without permission from Central Press and Printing Secretary.

13. Publication Department is responsible for the propagation of Shrii *P. R. Sarkar's* ideas through the media of publication. It supervises the implementation of the central publication policies. It acts as the distribution agent for all of Shrii *P. R. Sarkar* books printed in English in India. It is also responsible to see that other *Ananda Marga* publications are printed with quality and distributed to all units of *Ananda Marga* in the sector. Efforts should be made to place all books into the popular bookstores and encourage public sale through book fairs and other methods of promotion, i.e. advertisement, mail-order catalogue etc.

14. Public Relations Department is responsible to create a

proper image of *Ananda Marga* in the eyes of the public, to establish cordial relationships with government agencies, newspapers, radio and television stations and to submit regular press releases promoting all AM functions. Likewise, up-to-date name and address lists should be kept on file of all important contacts. PR department is responsible for any legal cases that may arise within the organization's department. PR department is responsible for the proper telephonic network within and with other sectors. PR department supervises the legal papers for all vehicles within the sector. The PR department is also responsible for issuing a monthly newsletter and sending it to all AM offices in the sector.

15. Renaissance Universal acts as the platform for professionals, executives, scientists, artists, journalists, lawyers, academicians, students and other groups of thinkers. The purpose is to unite these intellectuals on common issues of concern like universal ideas, environmental awareness, social dynamism and moral development, etc. Another purpose is to hold symposiums, seminars and conferences to stimulate a collective effort to solve basic problems. RU clubs are to be started in every unit with reports submitted regularly. Aims of RU clubs are:

    a. To promote individual and collective ethical responsibility for applying science, arts, etc. for the welfare of the planet.
    b. To propagate the ideals of self-realization and social changes.
    c. To help and relieve the sufferings of the oppressed and disadvantaged.
    d. Organize debates, essay competitions, research work, etc.
    e. Distribute clothes, books and medical aid to the poor.
    f. Work for the establishment of guaranteed basic necessities: food, clothes, shelter, medical care, and education.

    RAWA or Renaissance Artists and Writers Association is a branch of RU which deals with artists and writers who wish to use their talents to promote art for service and blessedness. The aims of RAWA are:

    g. To develop the finer faculties of the human mind through music, drama, song, dance, poetry, etc.
    h. To encourage the subtler branches of art.
    i. To bring change in social values through art.
    j. To establish RAWA clubs enabling artists of all types to socialize, communicate, organize concerts, art exhibitions, research new art forms, etc.
    k. To help purchase equipment to be used by the members of RAWA.
    l. To organize fund-raising programs.
    m. To protect the artists from commercial exploitation.

16. Society Building Department gives importance to building a healthy society by encouraging and guiding proper family life based on spiritual ideals, as well as encouraging unmarried young men and women to dedicate their lives as whole-time workers of *Ananda Marga*. The society building department organizes seminars on child-rearing, cooperative living and other topics of concern for family margiis. SB propagates the social codes of *Ananda Marga* through Caryacarya I, II and III. SB organizes weekend retreats for youth in order to inspire them for a lifetime of service to humanity.

    *Ananda Marga* encourages revolutionary marriages (RM) for the purpose of breaking down all forms of social barriers. The spouses should be of different race, religious belief or national background.

17. The Social Security Department works primarily to provide the basic necessities of life to everyone in society through its implementation of the cooperative system:

    a. Food coops (producer and consumer)
    b. Cloth coops (producer and consumer)
    c. Readers coops (producer and consumer)
    d. Medical care coops
    e. Construction coops (producer and consumer)
    f. Credit coops, insurance coops etc.
    g. Medical care coops

18. The Women's Welfare Department works for the all-round development and upliftment of women in society. There are five sections of WWD.

a. WWD - General works to start schools, open homes, and centers for tribal women and unwed mothers, start medical relief for women, open women's retiring homes, open skills training centers for women and all-round relief for women.

b. GV - Girl's volunteers organizes camps in order to train young women in self-discipline, sense of collective responsibility, courage, valor, selflessness, self-esteem, fearlessness and willingness to fight against all obstacles.

c. HPMG (L) - Hnri *Pari Mandal Goshti* (ladies managed) works to promote spirituality through social functions like *Ananda Mela*, full moon festivals, *Katha Kiirtan* parties, *Akhand Kiirtan* parties, *Nagar Kiirtans*, music, dance and song competitions, mass initiations, organizing service programs in all units, as well as starting schools.

d. PWSA - Progressive Women's Spiritual Association organizes progressive-minded women into a spiritually oriented association in order to promote cardinal human values in society. They hold seminars, symposiums, conferences, parades, rallies, and other public gatherings to promote all-round development of society.

e. AMURTEL - *Ananda Marga* Universal Relief Team (Ladies managed) works in close cooperation with AMURT in the area of disaster relief. In time of calamity, volunteers are rushed to the scene with food, medicine, clothes, and other relief supplies. AMURTEL members specialize in other areas of health, education by sponsoring mother's courses and hygiene classes to indigents.

19. The ERAWS Department, Education Relief and Welfare Section is composed of five sections with their branches:

### 1. EDUCATION - Aims and objectives:
a. Spread the ideals of *Ananda Marga* education, b. Make the guidelines and policies of *Ananda Marga* schools, c. Produce books and educational materials for AM schools, d. Inspect AM schools, e. Train teachers for AM schools.

The education section of ERAWS is divided into three branches:
1. E1 - To organize and open schools, E2 - To control the schools standard through the *Ananda Marga Gurukula*, E3 - To publish educational material for AM schools and control examinations under the guidance and supervision of *Ananda Marga Gurukula*.

**Ananda Marga has three types of School:**
a. **AMSAI** - *Ananda Marga* Social Academic Institution. AMSAI begins with nursery and kindergarten and develops up to the University level, serving middle-class society.
b. **AMJAS** - *Ananda Marga Jagrti* School. AMJAS begins with nursery and kindergarten and develops up to primary levels serving the poorer section of society, with either no tuition or nominal fees, every AM *Jagrti* should start AMJAS.
c. **AMPES** - *Ananda Marga* People Schools. AMPES are for mass education for adults in basic literacy, hygiene, morality, etc.

### 2. Relief - Aims and objectives:
a. All temporary relief work, b. Open cheap kitchens, c. Visit hospitals, prisons, d. Keep relief stores ready to help in service work and during disaster, e. To carry out *Paiñca Seva* (five services):
1. General Relief gives basic necessities of life, i.e. food, clothes, medical care, shelter, and educational materials.
2. Medical Relief - distribution of medicines, special food for sick, baby food, open free medical clinics and camps.
3. *Narayana Seva Sadan* - help in calamity - help people with subsidized relief programs like cheap kitchen etc.
4. *Narayan Seva Vishesha* - help in calamity - helps people with free aid programs.
5. Aid for poor students helping with fees, clothes, books, accommodation.

homes etc.
b. To organize, maintain, supervise and inspect all permanent relief projects that do not come within the scope of R1, R2, and R3 as such drug crisis centers, halfway houses for youth, prisoners, mentally ill, ambulatory handicapped, etc.
c. Register as a permanent relief organization with the local government.
d. Contact government and private funding agencies and arrange independent sources of income.

### MEDICAL - Aims and Objective
The Medical section of ERAWS works to bring medical care to all people in need and to provide education so that people can live long and healthy lives. This work is accomplished by:
a. Assisting Relief Department in the establishment of medical camps and clinics and alternative medicines i.e. Homeopathy, Ayurvedic, Naturopathy, Acupressure, Yogic Treatments, Chinese traditional and herbal medicines.
b. Printing newsletters supporting alternative medical treatments.
c. Holding conferences, symposiums and other programs aimed at educating the public about medicine and medical treatments available.

NOTE: When a Margii asked Shrii P.R. Sarkar which has the best system of medicine, Shrii P.R. Sarkar replied that the system which cures the patient is the best.

### PCAP & CL - Prevention of Cruelty to Animals and Plants and Cheap Literature
Ananda Marga exists not only for the benefits of all people, but also for plants and animals. Shrii P.R. Sarkar explained in PCAP guidelines, "This universe is the patrimony of all living beings, because all are children of the same cosmic father. But the sky-licking greed of man always tries to deprive his brothers from their right to live in accordance with their inborn instincts and natural wants. The PCAP program is to bring world-wide consciousness among human beings, regarding the rights of the members of the animal and plant kingdom.

#### PCAP & CL - Aims and Objectives:
a. Protect endangered species of animals and plants,
b. create a sentiment of love for animals and plants by popularizing service to plants and animals,
c. Popularizing vegetarian diet,
d. Popularize tree planting,
e. Print cheap literature and distribute widely,
f. organize seminar, symposiums, parades, rallies, walkathons, radio and TV interviews, Press Releases,
g. Start movements among the margiis.

### 5. TPW & EMS - Tribal People's Welfare & Ek Manava Samaja
According to Shrii P.R. Sarkar's social philosophy, the standard of a society must be measured by the development of the weakest members. A truly progressive society means that the poorest members are improving their socio-economic condition, not that the rich are getting richer and the poor are getting poorer.

TPW deals with minority groups, i.e.:
a. Native inhabitants (Aborigines of Australia, the Red Indians of North America, Eskimos of the Arctic) who have been forced off their ancestral lands and divorced from the original lifestyle by intrusion of exploitative, colonial interests.
b. Cultural groups who are discriminated against due to their minority status i.e. the blacks of USA, the Jews of Russia, the Tibetans of China, and the Harijans of India etc.

Immigrants and foreigners from other lands who need education, guidance and protection from exploitation. Tribal peoples like the Masai and Bantu of East Africa, the Hottentots and Bushmen of South Africa, the Mayan, Incas and Aztecs of Central and South America, who require assistance in adjusting

Relief section is divided into three branches:

* **R1** - To open and maintain free hostels, children's homes, leper homes, homes for blind and invalid people.
* **R2 (AMURT)** - *Ananda Marga* Universal Relief Team, to start disaster relief operations.
* **R3 (AMUPRESO)** - *Ananda Marga* Universal Permanent Relief Society, to open permanent relief stores and to provide *Pañca Seva* permanently.

#### AMURT - Aims and objectives

a. To select volunteers and train them in first aid, firefighting, rescue, etc.
b. Organize relief stores in AMURT units (all units).
c. Affiliate AMURT with local government and international relief agencies.
d. Recruit doctors and nurses as volunteers.
e. Create teams of volunteers to be on call.
f. Secure all necessary equipment and supplies for effectively and promptly responding to any natural or man-made calamities: Vehicles, uniforms, supplies, radios, tools, megaphones, etc.
a. Maintain an AMURT account and organize fundraising efforts before and during emergencies.
b. Maintain files and other office records including letters of appreciation, news clippings, documents, reports, etc.
c. To respond to natural and man-made calamities with prompt and efficient relief in cooperation with government and other relief agencies. To see that no one suffering from a calamity is left with the feeling that the Almighty Father has forsaken them.
d. To obtain proper appreciation letters from government and local authorities benefited.

#### AMUPRESO - Aims and objectives

a. To organize, maintain, supervise and inspect all relief projects of permanent nature coming within the scope of R1, R2, R3, including children's homes, students' homes, cheap hostels, homes for the blind, handicapped people's homes, nursing to 20th century living.

Suggested guidelines for the implementation of this program:

a. To inspire these groups with the feeling of unity with the rest of the community by inviting them to various social functions, collective social service projects, cultural festivals, etc.
b. To render any and all service to these groups, assist them with their legal rights, assist them in tutorial classes in English, reading, writing, etc., defending them from verbal abuses, protect their human rights whenever they are exploited.
c. To establish TPW centers with at least 50 hectares of land whereon kinder, primary, and high schools would be built and started, children's homes, free medical clinic as well as collective farming.
d. To encourage maximum interaction of cultural groups, i.e. dancing, picnics, sports, games, theater, food, festivals, etc. Encourage minorities to study their history as a developing people and popularize the glories of their forefathers and their heroes.

### EMS - EK MANAVA SAMAJA - One Human Society

This organization is specifically given to popularize the sentiment of Universalism. EMS Aims and Objectives:

a. Organize symposiums of groups of various faiths and beliefs to discuss topics which all find common ground, for example, ecology.
b. Encourage the use of English as the worldwide medium of exchange amongst different language groups.
c. Popularize the metric system of measurement throughout the world.
d. Popularize the use of Roman script in different languages.
e. Encourage common festivals, common social functions,

f. Encourage marriages between people living in different corners of the world,
g. Encourage peoples to learn as many languages as possible,
h. Propagate the concept of universal inheritance and universal goal in collective gatherings,
i. Encourage all to strengthen human fraternity.

# VI

## ACTION PROGRAMME OF MY MASTER

> "Ours is a subjective approach and an objective adjustment—the happy blending between objective world and subjective goal. While moving towards the subjective goal, you must maintain adjustment with the objective world. There is no other alternative. When the human society is ensconced in this supreme idea, in the very near future it will become more constructive, with proper planning. Humanity of the entire universe will unite, and we are anxiously waiting for that happy moment."

> [Illustration representing the "Six Spokes of Life" in Ananda Marga:
> - **Central Hub:** A wheel labeled "ANANDA MARGA" with six spokes: *Preceptor*, *Spiritual Philosophy*, *Spiritual Technique*, *Socio-economic Philosophy*, *Social Outlook*, and *Scripture*.
> - **Left tracks:** Labeled *ASTI [Existence] Minimum requirement for Life: P-R-O-U-T*, *BHATI [growth]*, and *ANANDAM [Bliss]*.
> - **Brain waves:** Illustrated with numbers 1 to 4 representing: *1. Beta wave - restless*, *2. Alpha wave - calm*, *3. Theta wave - deep meditation*, *4. Cosmic consciousness*.
> - **Bottom banner:** *Human existence is Ideological flow* | *Morality is base* | *Intuition is the way* | *Life Divine is the goal*.
> - **Top-right:** An image of a person sitting in meditation next to a candle on a table.]

## Six Spokes of Life

Ananda Marga is a socio-spiritual organization. Spiritually it can not develop and spread in collective life without a well-knit socio-economic supra-structure. So Ananda Marga has laid down a set of strict rules and regulations for personal and social behavior in an ideal society. These rules include social norms, laws for personal and collective behavior as well as for social functions. These rules are progressive in nature, rational in thought, scientific in outlook and neo-humanistic in spirit. There is no room for superstition or dogma in its outlook.

Some aspects of this social outlook of Ananda Marga have been discussed below.

**Society:** Human Society is one and indivisible. No barrier of caste, creed, religion, race, nationality or language should divide this universal human society. There is only one race and its name is the human race. There is only one society, which is the Universal Human Society.

**Human Being:** Every human being is a Divine Child. Human existence is trifarious - Physical, mental, and spiritual. With a balanced and integrated development of those three spheres a person will develop a total and balanced personality.

**Man and Women:** Both are equal in status and enjoy the same dignity. Both as equal members of the society enjoy equal social rights in all walks of life and will be required to undertake responsibility in accordance with their potentialities and physical capacities.

Shrii *P.R. Sarkar*, came with a revolutionary and radical

philosophy and ideology discarding all the narrow dogmatic concepts. To Him human society is one and indivisible. He proclaims, "*Paramapurusa* is our father, *Parama Prakrti* is our mother and we are the members of this cosmic family". Therefore, to establish a human society that is universal, there should be the creation of a great world and for the creation of a great world, there should be the formation of world government. For the formation of world government there is a need for collective psychology, which is the historical interpretation of human society, not the class struggle. For the creation of collective psychology, Shrii *P.R. Sarkar* says six factors are required. They are:

1. Spiritual Philosophy, 2. Spiritual Cult, 3. Socio-economic Theory, 4. Social Outlook, 5. Scripture, 6. Preceptor.

Out of these six factors, if any factor is absent, the society will die a premature death. This has happened in all the dead and dying civilizations of the world. Some of them are as follows:

1. Over one thousand five hundred years ago, Arabs were developed but they were defeated by the *Islamic* wave and lost their glory.
2. Egypt was developed in art and architecture but was defeated by the *Arabs*. Today's Egypt is the Egyptian form of pro-Islamic Arabian civilization.
3. The Roman or Latin civilization was highly developed, but it also withered away because it had no social outlook or no Socio-economic theory. Besides, theirs was a fascist mentality and the slave mentality and system was rampant there.
4. The Aryans defeated Non-Aryans.
5. Today, Marxism societies are also dead due to the same fact.

Marx had not considered all the factors. He laid stress on the economic factors only. Engels realized this mistake and in a letter to Bloch, written in 1890, he writes "Marx and I were partly responsible for the fact that at times our disciples have laid more weight upon the economic factor than belongs to it. We were compelled to emphasize its central character in opposition to our opponents who denied it, and there was not always the time, place, and occasion to do justice to the other factors in the reciprocal interactions of these historical processes".

Witnessing this historical incident, Shrii Sarkar considered and expounded six factors for the establishment of an ideal human society.

To organize and unite people on a common platform, a common sentiment is required. Only the spiritual sentiment is common to all. So for the society, one common spiritual philosophy is needed and Shrii Sarkar propounded *Ananda Marga* spiritual philosophy, which has been embraced all over the globe.

Spiritual Techniques - Shrii *P.R. Sarkar* gave a rational bio-psycho-spiritual process to realize the philosophical conclusions, to cover the five rudimental factors into ectoplasmic stuff and then into consciousness. It helps in psychic dilation and spiritual elevation. With this practical *Sadhana*, he attracted the lakhs of people in the world in the bond of love and universal brotherhood.

Socio-Economic Theory - For a happy socio-economic living, Shrii Sarkar gave a perfect economic theory on the basis of rational distribution and maximum utilization of the entire potentialities of the universe. The theory is known as PROUT, which is the only panacea of the present world's problems and this theory is included in many universities of the world.

Social Outlook - To inculcate the feeling of cosmic brotherhood, *Shrii* Sarkar declared, "We are all the progenies of the supreme Progenitor. The cosmic entity is our father, The Cosmic Operative Principle is our mother, and we are all brothers and sisters". To create unbiased well-directed social consciousness, Shrii Sarkar propounded the theory of Neo-Humanism with the following factors:

### Caryacarya (Part-1)

**Infant Ja'tkarma - The first feeding of solid food and naming of an Infant** - When an infant is between six months to one year old his Ja'takarma ceremony is performed. At least five *Gurubhra'ta's* will sit together and the infant will be laid before them. Thereafter, the Acarya (or the oldest person present) will recite the following verse first and all present will follow:

*Onm'Madhuva'ta rta'yate, Madhuks'arantu sindhavah,*
*Ma'dhviirn'ah santovos'adhi, Madhunaktamutas'aso,*
*Madhumat Pa'rthivam'Rajah, Madhuma'nno vanaspati*
*Madhuma'n astu suryo, Ma'dhvirgavo bhavantu nah,*
*Ohm Madhu madhu madhu*

The meaning - May the wind bring blessings with it! May the ocean yield felicity! Our herbs are blissful! Day and night be sweet! May the dust particles be blissful! May the Devaloka and Pitraloka prove sweet! May our plants be charming! May the sun shower felicity! May our animals (domestic) be blessed! Brahma Madhu, *madhu, madhu,* Brahma Madhu."

After doing this, they will speak in the mother tongue or in a language intelligible to all those present: "O gracious Brahma, may we unitedly be able to arrange for the upkeep, treatment and physical progress of this infant has this day entered our society amongst us."

Thereafter, everyone present will take one tumblerful of water (cool or lukewarm, in accordance with the season) and pour the same into a big vessel and then recite again:

*Onm'Madhuva'ta rta'yate kadhuks'arantu singhavah...*

Merciful Lord, may we be able to provide adequate education for the mental development of this infant who has this day entered our society amongst us.

After that, all persons present will pour a tumblerful of water in the same manner and recite:

*Onm' Madhuva'ta rta'yate madhuks' arantu ...* "O gracious *Brahma*, may we succeed in arranging for the spiritual progress through proper education of this infant who has entered our society amongst us this day."

Then in the aforesaid manner, again one tumblerful of water will be poured each into the same big vessel and the following will be recited:
