"The ills perpetuated in society are in a great measure created by the exploiters themselves. To swell their bank accounts, the exploiters create an artificial scarcity of food, clothes, and other necessary consumer goods, and loot profits in the black market. Those who have no buying capacity for high-priced food and clothes take to stealing and other shady methods in order to meet their bare necessities. These ill-fed, ill-clad flocks of poor wretches work for these greedy black marketers and smugglers. When apprehended, it is they who are penalized - the rich escape on the strength of their influence. Throwing

their conscience to the four winds, these poor wretches take to sin and crime still more desperately. The society accuses these sinners and criminals, while the rich exploiters enact the role of popular leaders, wear garlands, let loose fireworks of speeches, and exhort the masses with a clarion call to make more sacrifices."

Thus, it is not that there is a scarcity of resources; rather it is the misuse and distribution of resources that creates suffering.

"It must always be remembered that the value of money lies in its use. If more money is accumulated than is necessary, it becomes valueless for lack of use. The money you keep idle and valueless makes you responsible for the injustice done to the hungry and naked. Your valueless hoard shall have to be made valuable by providing opportunity for others use."

Money, for my master, must be reinvested productively, with a concern for the public, not for national prestige or fame.

"Economics teaches that the 'rolling of money' should never be blocked by any sort of non-productive investment. Sometimes it is found that business persons misuse loans to construct an unnecessary building for their business or a new show room, and thus prevent the possibility of reinvesting the capital and increasing their wealth. Economics teaches that money taken on loan for investment in business should always be utilized for that purpose, and should never be utilized in any unproductive venture. Thus the investment of foreign loans, for example, should never be used in constructing large railway stations instead of contracting railway lines."

Banking also must be organized for the people.

"First, a system of banking should be organized in such a way that the life of the average people will not be jeopardized by the demonic greed of the banks. In the past, in most countries of the world, banks forced people to pay exorbitant interest rates. Even today, this still takes place, not only in the undeveloped countries, but also in developing and developed countries. Violation of this point could bring ruination not only to low and middle-income groups but also to the wealthy sections of society."

However, economics, as Sarkar has developed in His four-fold division, implicitly include the role of the state. In most cases, the state exists not for the general public, but for various elites.

"Whatever the quantity of production in the country, production in itself doesn't enhance the standard of living of the people. The rich become richer and get the scope to continue their exploitation mercilessly. The rulers of the state in state capitalism get themselves entrenched in the role of exploiters with a far tighter grip, capitalism in any form-whether individual capitalism or collective capitalism - ultimately becomes more dangerous and blood-sucking than the blood thirsty ghosts and demons."

Self-Reliance- To counter the efforts of capitalists, *Sarkar* argues for self-reliance, a reinvestment based on cooperative structure where development leads to the realization of the need for local economies.

"But for this industrial revolution, we must not depend upon the raw materials bought from foreign countries. You should remember that the development of any country must not depend on importing raw materials. For this development we must utilize the indigenous raw materials - materials available in the country itself. Those who love society, those who love the people of the country and are keen to bring about socio-economic elevation of the country, must think in terms of an industrial revolution based on the materials available in their own economic unit".

"We cannot neglect a single creature, nor can we ignore any particular part of the world. Therefore, it is desirable to pursue the policy of decentralization in the management of industry as much as possible. The promotion of industry in one part of the

world cannot eradicate either poverty or unemployment in any other part. So it is desirable to form self-sufficient units one by one, to produce the essential commodities of life, at least in the fields of agriculture and industry. Otherwise, people may have to face the tremendous hardship and misery during war and other abnormal circumstances. With the development of transport facilities, we can increase the scope of these units."

However, as opposed to other self-reliance perspectives, my master is not anti-technology or anti-growth (He does not believe that population is the cause of underdevelopment or overdevelopment as previously illustrated). For Him, technology depends on the structure of the economy and on the culture of the society, whether it is materialistic or spiritual. "Under a capitalist framework, (mechanization) means more misery and unemployment of the common people. Doubling the yield by using machines will decrease the required number of laborers by half. Consequently, capitalists will lay off workers mercilessly. The unemployed laborers are ruined, bit by bit, by poverty and hunger. A few of them try to keep their souls and bodies together by indulging in theft, corruption and other anti social activities. This situation is certainly not desirable. No such reaction is possible in a collective economic system. There, mechanization will mean less labor and more comforts. With a double increase in productivity of machines, the working hours will be reduced by half."

This half reduction in working hours may be further reduced in the future.

"The proper use of science under a collective economic system will only bring about human welfare. It may well be possible that due to mechanization, no one will have to labor for more than five minutes a week! Being not always engrossed in anxiety about food and clothes, humanity will not misuse its mental and spiritual wealth. People will be able to devote more time to sports, literary discourses and spiritual pursuits."

Self-reliance is particularly important in agriculture. *Sarkar* presents suggestions on how the structure can be transformed so as to achieve self-reliance and self-sufficiency.

"According to PROUT, first land holdings must be economically viable to facilitate increased production. Here an economic holding means a holding where the minimum input brings the maximum output. It is not possible to predetermine the size of an economic holding, because factors like the fertility of the soil, climatic conditions, etc. in relation to input, output, productivity, etc. will determine the optimum size of the holding. Today, many people believe that increased production is possible if land holdings are small. But this thinking is defective because production is increased and when farm managers take correct and timely decisions. If managers are very efficient, then even very large farms can increase production. Of course, it is not necessary that all farms should be large. The main thing is that the holding should be economically viable... Economic holdings should comprise land of the same topography, have uniform irrigation, and other standardized facilities. The sizes of economic holdings should be progressively increased, keeping all these factors in mind. Thus the size of economic holdings will vary from country to country".

Agriculture cannot flourish under a capitalist system.

"In Prout's agricultural system there is no place for intermediaries. Those who invest their capital and derive a profit from the productive labors of others are capitalists. Capitalists thrive on the blood of industrial and agricultural laborers like parasite. Those who act as intermediaries in the agricultural sector are agricultural capitalists. They get their land cultivated by others but they misappropriate the produce of the land."

This is not to say that the mechanization is unimportant.

"PROUT advocates maximum modernization in agriculture and industry. In the cooperative agricultural system, modern

equipment must be utilized. Such modernization will facilitate increased production."

"PROUT advocated a decentralized economy, so policies must be adopted to utilize all local resources to accelerate and diversify development. This will ensure development throughout a country, and not just in a few regions. To achieve this aim, people must first be employed agricultural co-operatives. In modern India, there are two kinds of places. In one place, there is a surplus of labor and in the other, there is a deficit of labor. However, the very concept of deficit labour is a relative one. Where there are inadequate opportunities for proper economic development, there is surplus labor. All undeveloped economic regions are areas of surplus labor. When surplus labor moves to another region, then the undeveloped region has every chance of remaining undeveloped forever.

According to PROUT, top priorities must be given to finding employment for surplus labor in undeveloped regions. This policy will raise the standard of living of the people in the undeveloped area. If this policy is not implemented and surplus labor is allowed to move to other regions and the Marxist policy that "those who sow shall reap" is followed, and then all tea plantations, coal mines, and other natural resources will be controlled by outside labor. Local people will lose control over their natural resources. This is very clear. The children of the soil must have priority in employment opportunities. As long as there is no full employment for local people, every effort must be made to employ them. Until the local labor is fully employed, no new development programs should be started. Scandinavian countries did not commence any new development programs for this reason while creating employment for the local people, consideration must be given to local sentiments. For instance, in certain provinces of India, there is surplus intellectual labour. People in this category are ready to work as clerks on the low monthly wage of Rs. 90 (about US$ 2) but they are not prepared to work as porters and earn more money. The problem of surplus intellectual labor is a special one and should be solved in a proper way. In industries in such regions with surplus intellectual labor, the less physical labor employed the better. Thus development programs should be formulated to time, place and person of different socio-economic regions."

While *Sarkar* does not spend a great deal of time discussing appropriate indicators of development and wealth, He does critique per capita income, a common indicator.

"Question..... What do we need, increasing per capita income or increasing purchasing capacity" Answer..... PROUT suggests that increases in per capita income are not a sufficiently reliable and scientific index to determine the standard and progress of a particular and deceitful, because it refers to a simple mathematical calculation of total national income divided by total population. This does not give the correct picture of a particular socio-economic unit, as the wealth disparity in society is concealed. Per capita income shows the mean and not the variation of income distribution. If inflation is also considered, the reliability of per capita income is further reduced.

On the other hand, purchasing capacity is the real index of how a person's economic needs can be met by their income. All Prout's plans and programs in the socio-economic sphere should be aimed at increasing purchasing capacity of the people. PROUT stresses increasing purchasing capacity and not per capita income. Per capita income is not a proper indication of the increase in the standard of living of the people because people may have very high incomes and still may not be able to purchase the necessities of life. On the other hand, if per capita income is low, but people have great purchasing capacity, they are much better off. So purchasing capacity not per capita income is the true measure of economic prosperity. Everyone's requirements should be within their pecuniary periphery or

purchasing capacity."

My master on social justice—To appreciate my master's thoughts on social justice, it is important to understand His concept of the nature of society. A key element of His outlook is the notion of a universal family.

"All properties of this earth, movable as well as immovable, belong to no particular individual, everything is the patrimony of all of us and we all have God as our father. Every living being can utilize his rightful share of his property, as does the member of a joint family favored with rights by the father.

Like the members of a joint family, we should not only safeguard, but also use the properties of the universe in a proper way, in feeding and clothing ourselves so that everyone is guaranteed equal rights. Let us establish a system in which all of us may live, each with a sound mind and a sound body.

We should not forget even for a moment that this whole animate world is a large family in which nature has not assigned any property to any individual. Selfish opportunists have created individual ownership so that they might take advantage of the defects of this system in order to grow fatter in a parasitic way. When the all creatures have inherited the whole property of this universe, how then can there be any justification for a system in which someone receives a flow of huge excess, while others die for a lack of a handful of grain? Every member in a joint family is provided with the necessary food, clothing, education, medical aid, and other amenities according to the economic resources of the entire family. If, however, any member of the family takes possession of more grain, clothes, books, or medicines than he actually requires, will it be bring distress to the other members of the family? In such circumstances, his act will certainly be anti-religious and anti-social".

Although human beings have been on earth for a very long time, this feeling of a global family has never emerged.

"This innate spirit of the word 'Samaj', society is to move together. If we take into account the proper spirit of the word, we may unhesitatingly assert that man has not yet learned to build a society worthy of the name."

Social Injustice—Throughout human history, divisiveness and exploitation have alienated people from one another. Large groups of people have been placed in subordinated roles and have had their human rights abridged. Women have suffered greatly in this way.

"As in most other living species, in the human society. females are physically weaker than males, Nevertheless; their value in society is not a bit less than that of men. Selfish men. however, have exploited feminine weakness to the limit. Although they publicly declare women to be a community of mothers, they have actually relegated them to the level of domesticated cattle and sheep. In every sphere of life, these men have either substantially curtailed women's rights, or have kept them wholly subservient to men's whims and caprices."

The subordination of women has not only affected them but the whole society as well.

"There is no chance for the welfare of the world unless the condition of women is improved. It is not possible for a bird to fly on one wing."

"Poor handicapped, and illiterate people have likewise suffered the pain and humiliation created by divisiveness. Instead of acknowledging their worth and helping them to overcome their problems, society has treated them as outcasts.

What we see in the market place and railway stations is the exhibition of half-clad street beggars and lepers all stretching out their begging bowls the only means of earning their livelihood. They are fortunate if anybody contemptuously flings a copper coin as someone walks past them. Their shrill cries fall on deaf ears only. On the other hand, the affluence of foods kept ready in luxurious houses to entertain the big guns of society ridicule the present human civilization."

There are also divisions in society created along geographic, culturally and racial lines. Such social disparities further server to inhibit the collective progress of humanity. Another group of outcastes is the so-called sinners or criminals. Yet, you people are often goaded into anti-social behavior.

"Suppose you see a man steal something. If you see externally, you will see the man was stealing. He should be punished. Shouldn't he be punished? Yes. he should be punished. This is ordinary seeing. But if you apply your inner vision, i.e. intuitional sight, you see that the man was without food for four days, and so under pressure of circumstances, he had to steal. So after this inner seeing, this inner vision, you will come to the decision that we will remodel the socio-economic structure so that never is he compelled to steal under pressure of circumstances, because of being hungry for several days."

In that setting, my master sees a very different criminal.

"The path that lies before thieves who steal out of need is extremely narrow. The solution wholly depends on the strong economic foundation of the state concerned and of the whole world. If anybody is to be blamed for the poverty and social injustice that leads to crime, we will have to blame every national leader in the world. Those who make beasts of people by making them victims of circumstances, to my mind, are the very people to stand trial if anyone is to be tried and judged at all."

Social Justice- My master's vision is to include all people in the universal family and to make sure that all move ahead together. Women and men must be able to share fully the rights and responsibilities of social life.

"Every person must understand that for the maintenance of any organism, there must be a close cooperation between each of its component parts. Humanity is not inert and the relationships between human beings depend on more than cooperation. This cooperation, instead of being based on a master-servant relationship, must be constructed in a warmly cordial atmosphere of free human beings. It should be coordinated cooperation and not a subordinated one."

Petty self-interest needs to be replaced with a sense of universalism wherein all members of society are accorded respect. The basic physical necessities of life must also be guaranteed to everyone so they will not feel compelled to resort to immorality for their survival.

In the case of crime, my master advocates a common criminal code and an emphasis on reforming criminals.

"There should be one universal legal and criminal code for the universal humanity. It is not at all desirable that there be different laws for different people, different countries and different communities. All laugh in happiness, cry in sorrow, and beat their breast in mourning. Why must we separate human from human with a thousand and one imaginary distinctions." It is not nature's desire that human beings should penalize one another. Whenever some action is taken to penalize someone, a sense of revenge or vindictiveness arises in the mind of the dispenser of justice and stifles the judge in him or her. Hence, I suggest that the words 'penal code' should be eliminated from the social life of humanity. If anybody, whether a judge someone else, it should be corrective not penal. Then the offender, no matter how great his or her guilt, will have no cause for accusing anyone. Regardless of flaws in judgment, it will not harm him or her in any way."

It is also important that we have constitutional structures, which guarantee fundamental rights, such as spiritual freedom and educational opportunity. These constitutions should also safeguard our cultural legacy and indigenous cultural expression, such as language. Consideration must be given as well to the rights other creatures. Srii sarkar has prepared a Neo-Magna Carta to serve as a basis of these constitutions.

"Neo-Magna Carta is far more developed, neo-humanistic

universal and progressive than the old one. In the near future, the World Government will be formed and it will have a world constitution. The Neo-Magna Carta will be one of the major corner stones of the constitution."

My master believes that a spiritual perspective is very important in the development of social justice.

"The Cosmic Ideal alone can be the unifying force, which shall strengthen humanity to smash the bondages and abolish all narrow domestic walls of fissiparous tendencies.

The Supreme Consciousness (*Parama Purusa*) is the goal of everyone. We are all moving towards Him, both individually and collectively, but we will not be able to move ahead if we compromise with all the disparities of collective life. We should eradicate all these inequalities, while at the same time, we move together towards our spiritual goal. The progress of all would be accelerated if the inequalities were uprooted and it is this that should be the motivation behind our movement towards the supreme."

The effort to develop and maintain a broad minded perspective is an ongoing one.

"To keep the waves of that vibration active, one has to make adequate arrangements for moral education. To keep that vibration flowing all the time, a never-ending fight in both individual as well as collective life must continue. One must be vigilant lest time dull the edge of the vital force, lest dullness steal over courage, lest inertia sneak into the mind."

It is a great and noble endeavor; one which *P.R. Sarkar* says is truly worthwhile.

"When we have embraced the ideal of the principle of social equality, when we have attained the physical strength, psychic intellect, and spiritual wisdom to materialize it - should we not play a successful role implement this noble and sublime idea? Our coming to this earth and our remaining here, our very breath, every vibration of our existence - should this not attain fulfillment? By utilizing all our physical, psychic, and spiritual powers, should we not attain the pinnacle of human glory?"

Books and Writings by My Master on Social Justice:
The Human Society Parts 1 and 2, Problems of the Day, The Liberation of Intellect - Neo-Humanism, Idea and Ideology, Caryacarya Part 2, Thoughts of P.R. Sarkar, The Three Causes of Sin, Social Value and Human Cardinal Principle.

My Master on Women's Right - "There is no chance for the welfare of the world unless the condition of women is improved. It is not possible for a bird to fly on one wing. We, men and women, are the progeny of the same Supreme consciousness. Women and men are equally divine and inherit similar and equal rights of life, liberty, and expression.

They have their potentiality no less than men do. The difference in natural and biological characteristics between men and women speaks only of coordinated cooperation and not of subordinated cooperation."

He believed that women have always demonstrated their capabilities in society.

"In the annals of human history, we do find women whose memory glorifies not only womanhood, but the entire human world. In philosophy and spiritually, social reform and educational pursuits, science and technology, they stand second to none."

Indeed, He believed that women governed early societies. The matrilineal societies owed their cohesiveness to their women leaders. There came a time, though, when this changed and women began to be exploited by men.

"Yet the annals of history depict the sad and painful episodes of women's exploitation throughout the world. To fulfill this sinister design, dogmas were created which led to psycho-economic exploitation. Dogmas were cunningly popularized and women were degenerated to slavery. Selfish men, however, have exploited feminine weakness to the limit.

Although they publicly declare women to be a community of mothers, they have actually relegated them to the level of domesticated cattle and sheep. In every sphere of life, these men have either substantially curtailed women's rights or have kept them wholly subservient to men's whims and caprices."

My master saw this exploitation as having arisen on several fronts, including among others:

**Religion:**

"Psychic exploitation infused the minds of women and many symbols which are religiously observed are nothing but symbols of slavery. In many religions of the world today women are not allowed to become priests in the religious hierarchy.

It is a fact that in almost all scriptures and their notes and commentaries, it has been said in unambiguous language that women are not entitled to get salvation. Those vested interests, in the realm of religion, not spiritually, used to say this. They perpetuated their exploitation over females for an indefinite period of time. This was their psychology. That is why they used to say that salvation and emancipation are for males only."

**Economics:**

"Crippling them economically on pain of unrelenting social penalization and perdition in hell, the women were made so very dependent on men that the very sense of injustice of a man possessing many wives at the same time was effaced from their minds.

**Politics**

"Females are debarred from many socio-political rights. A few hundred years ago they had no voting rights in many countries of the world. Why? They are also human beings they also have the same right as men. Renaissance people will have to start a movement for social equality, and fight against such inequality based on sexual difference. Are women like cattle or sacks of cloth that people give as gifts at his time of marriage? Are they slaves to be sold to others? As long as women lacked courage or intellect, they tolerated these things—but what a great humiliation! To submit them to such indignities is far worse than to whip them publicly on the road. Are women simply bundles of cloth to be sent to the washer-man's house?"

**Languages:**

"Though the matter concerns grammar and it is not easy to change overnight yet the word 'man' in common gender includes both man and woman. Then why does not 'woman' also mean both man and woman? This is also lopsided justice. 'Man' is in masculine gender, but 'man' is also common gender."

**Regaining the Balance:**

My master rejected the present status of women in society and called out for a coordinated, cooperative leadership between men and women.

"Women must not be suppressed, and there should not be domination of males in society. Society should have a cooperative leadership, not a subordinated leadership, but a coordinated, cooperative leadership between males and females. If fifty percent of the population, the ladies, remains downtrodden how can there is all-round progress of the human society? These disparities, these inequalities will have to be rooted out of the society."

He believed that courageous women will once again play a key role in reestablishing their rights.

"One or two women who appeared to have been given the spiritual rights and opportunities, where not actually given, but they established their rights virtually by force on the strength of their own personalities. We stand to create a powerful, dynamic, and up surging social consciousnesses especially amongst women, so they are inspired to rise, awake, abolish dogma and annihilate all symbols of slavery and usher in a new era of coordinated cooperation and glorious achievement."

But Baba believed that men, as well as women, must be active in the struggle.

"Whatever responsibility there be on this account, it is entirely man's own. If any agitation is called for at all, the initiative must come from men themselves. Today, having recognized the necessity and interest of women, it now devolves on man to gradually restore to her rights, which one day she had surrendered to him in a weak moment of helplessness or in obedience to her inner bidding."

How can we measure success in this struggle?

"There are many who think that a few women who are made ministers or members of parliament will serve as the shining example of equal rights and feminine progress. But is this a correct outlook? A proper educational system is the only way to expedite progress. A woman of any country who becomes a minister or an ambassador is no index to the real status of the women in that country. To elevate women's dignity in society is neither so easy nor so cheap."

Rather, my master proposed a comprehensive program to reach the goal of social justice for women. These measures include:

1. Free education for all women in all countries of the world
2. No discrimination in the social, educational and religious realms
3. Dogmas should be abolished
4. Women must be provided economic equality and social security.

In His own organization, He accorded equality of responsibility to women and men.

"In our *Ananda Marga*, both men and women have equal responsibility. In the manner prescribed for marriage in our Marga, both the boy and girl have to take equal responsibility. Women and men are considered on the same footing and there is no question of men considering themselves superior to women."

My master believed that for true progress to occur in human society, women must play a co-equal role.

"If half the society is steeped in inertia and prejudice, the other half will undoubtedly find the journey tough going, since it must carry this beast of burden along. In individual life, purity is equally necessary for both men and women, and for that, real spiritual vision is needed. But this is impossible as long as injustice to either of the two sexes exists. Let womanhood be the vanguard of a new revolution which humanity must achieve for a glorious tomorrow."

### Ecology (Prama' & Master Unit)

Prama and Master Unit Essential items for all master units:

1. Model farm and agricultural extension service: Training in Baba's ideal farming for the surrounding population.
2. Seed bank and cheap seed distribution center: This center will collect good quality seeds (either cultivated or purchased from local farmers or the market) and provide at cheap rates to people.
3. Free plant distribution (tree nursery from seedlings): The following system should be used to prepare plants for distribution. Seedlings should be grown until they are one and one-half feet tall. They should then be uprooted and their roots soaked in water for half an hour. Next, the main root of each plant should be cut off one inch below the base of the plant, and the remaining roots should then be soaked in water for ten minutes. The plants should then be packed for distribution or immediately placed in the field. Plants that are prepared in this way will produce large, sweet fruits, better than those produced by seedlings, but not as well as those produced by grafted plants.
4. Flour Mill
5. Bakery
6. Dairy farm with production of dairy products
7. Biogas plant with dairy farm
8. Apiculture (Bee keeping)

9. Solar energy
10. School and children's home
11. Animal sanctuary
12. Medical service

Why Master Units? - All around us the world is collapsing. Religious hatred is erupting into terrible violence. The oil crisis threatens a total energy breakdown. Greed and speculation have precipitated an economic catastrophe. The Green revolution has created brown deserts; it has destroyed our soils, poisoned our bodies and shattered rural harmony.

Educational systems everywhere are crumbling as students are learning less and less and becoming more and more alienated than ever before. And ecological devastation is tearing our world - its plants, animals, water, and soil and sky apart.

At this critical juncture in earth's history, Baba has given the programme of master units as model for society in all respects: in agriculture, energy, industry, education, economy, ecology and spiritual development.

Action speaks louder than words. We may talk endlessly of PROUT, but people want to see PROUT materialized practically. This is exactly why Baba has given the master unit program. We must show in action our alternative system of Neo-Humanist education, our real 'Green Revolution' of Baba's ideal farming system, our industrial cooperatives, our systematic restoration of the environment, our development of alternative energy and our internal realization of cosmic oneness.

But our Master Units are not only the prototypes of the PROUT communities of the future. They are also self-sufficient survival centers for the coming years of turmoil. In time of famine they will provide food, in drought, water, in energy crisis, alternative sources of power, in economic depression, full employment. They will supply all the five essentialities of life (food, clothes, education, medical care, and shelter) during years of increasing want.

Baba's Ideal Farming System - "Amongst all the types of culture of the physical stratum, agriculture is the best. We must encourage and develop agriculture."

Farming includes:
* Agriculture (use of fertilizers, pest control, etc)
* Animal Husbandry
* Pisciculture (fish farming)

Agriculture is the production of staple crops, such as:
Pulses, cereals and coarse grains, oil seeds, vegetables, sugar crops (sugar cane, sugar beet, date palm, palmyra), spices, medicinal plants and herbs, special items i.e. cash crops like coffee, tea and cocoa.

### Self sufficiency

The goal is self-sufficiency. With inflation it becomes useless, so all our farming projects should strive to be self sufficient, not dependent on outside resources. Our integrated approach to farming must incorporate:

Cottage industries, energy production, research centers, water conservation.

### Special emergency survival program:

1. **Vegetables** - Vegetables are rich in vitamins and minerals, so they are very important component for human diet. People can be healthy without eating rice or bread but they cannot remain healthy if they do not eat vegetables. There are certain food items to be included for all integrated farming projects, vegetables are one of the items and potatoes another. Without vegetables, human beings are more prone to disease and nutritional problems. The best fertilizer for vegetables, especially green vegetables is rotten vegetables (compost). One quarter of the agricultural land is to be allotted for: wax gourd (petal), bottle gourd (lau), pumpkin (lal Kumra), bitter gourd (karaila) and cucumber. These are all-season vegetables. (Vegetable creepers growing in the

winter season do not require a platform)

2. Fruits (Horticulture): Fruits are a vital part of the human diet, the easiest food for humans to digest. Many fruits have a purifying and cleansing effect on the body and most fruits are high in vitamins and are very nutritious. Some fruits have medicinal value as well. Even in the emergency planting program, fruits should not be left out of the human diet completely, although cereals may be omitted. This demonstrates the significance of fruit.

System for planting fruit trees - big, medium, and small
1. Big Trees should be planted in an area of 15 feet by 15 feet
2. Medium Trees should be planted in the middle between two big trees (i.e. at a distance of 7 and half feet from two big trees).
3. Small Trees should be planted in between two big trees at a distance of every 5 feet (i.e. two small trees may be planted between two big trees)
4. Ginger, turmeric, and sweet potato should be planted in the shade of fruit trees in the prescribed rotation.

Some Big fruits trees: Mango, Walnut, Chestnut, Tamarind, Myrobalam, Almond and Cherry.
Medium Trees: Persimmon, Date palm, *Golap Jam*, Coconut, *Devaphalam*, Apricot.
Small Trees: Fig, Pistachio and Pomegranate.

The land should first be divided into blocks so that 25 big fruit trees can be planted in each block. Then as many medium and small fruit trees as possible should be planted in the remaining area. Plant one big tree, one medium tree etc after the medium trees have been planted; start planting small trees in between the big trees. Before the trees are planted deep pits should be dug and a good amount of compost and some calcium should be put into them. Calcium is useful in planting all types of fruit trees because it makes the fruit sweet.

Fruit trees conserve a lot of water in their fibrous root system and fruit. They can be planted as riverside trees and beside paddy and other fields to help conserve water.

Pulses - Pulses are more important for the human diet than cereals especially in emergencies. Most pulses are high in protein and have varying degrees of fat. For farming, pulses are important as nitrogen fixers. The roots of all pulses should be left in the soil for some time after harvest so that the nitrogenous compounds that are produced by their roots can help increase soil productivity. Pulses make excellent blended crops with plants like cereals that do not fix nitrogen in the soil.

Black gram - (*'Biro'* or 'Beulah' in Bengali, *'urada'* in *Hindi*) Black gram is the tastiest in gram and the most nutritious of all the pulses. It should be avoided only when one's stomach is not in order. When the gram crop is one month old, its leaves and stems should be picked for use as green vegetables. This produces offshoots so that there is more flowering and fruiting. But this practice of plucking leaves and stems should be stopped one month before flowering. (This rule applies to all pulses).

Black gram may also be grown as a blended crop with Turmeric, Sugarcane, Sag, Brinjal, Chili and Radish.

Soyabean - Soybean is 25% richer in protein than milk and 2 times more food value than *Seem* (long beans) and *Chana* (gram). The cultivation of soybean should be encouraged to meet people's nutritional requirements.

Potato - Potato contains carbohydrates and can be eaten as a substitute for cereal in emergency, they can be eaten instead of rice. Potato is especially beneficial when little land is available for cultivation and many people must be fed economically. To avoid pests, potato should be treated with mustard oil or castor cake. Cow dung is the best manure. Between the potatoes, radishes should be planted. The main root of the radish grows in such a way that it does not affect the potatoes. Along with these some small spices such as cumin, fenugreek etc. may be planted.

Fodder for cows - Some crops such as sweet potatoes and

black gram are good fodder for animals. They can be pruned regularly, thus encouraging more growth and providing more animal fodder. In addition, some crops need to be grown exclusively for fodder. One of the best grasses to encourage milk production in cows is Napier grass (*Pennisetum purpureum*). Wherever possible, this grass should be grown on the top of hillocks and hills to conserve the best farmland for other crops. This may be a bit difficult because grass needs lots of water, but the attempt should be made.

"All farms should start production of these items immediately. They are the minimum items necessary for physical survival. These items will ensure your survival in any difficult times that may come."

Herbs and medicinal plants should be given special importance. Some plants are very useful in helping to cure mental disorders. For example, Asiatic Pennywort (Brahmi sag or *Centella asiatica* (L)) increases memory power and is good for the brain. Bottle gourd (*Lau* or *Lagenaria siceraria* (L)) is good for the mentally disturbed. *Tulsi Nishanda* oil can be used to treat madness.

### Maximum Utilization of Land

**Blending of crops** – Our system of integrated farming is designed to utilize every inch of land, not only surface land, but also the space under the surface and even the space above the surface should be used to the maximum 100% (for example, ground nut under the surface and sesame above the ground).

There are three main systems of cropping which will ensure the maximum utilization of land: mixed cropping, supplementary cropping and crop rotation. In mixed cropping, two or more crops are grown in a field at the same time. This reduces soil erosion and wastage of agricultural land and makes better use of water. It also helps retain the fertility of the soil (legumes add nitrogen to the soil whereas maize consumes nitrogen, so these should be planted together). Well-selected plant combinations maintain soil fertility and structure.

In supplementary cropping, there is one main crop and a minor crop to support it. In crop rotation, the soil is under cultivation for most of the year.

Cultivation should be done on a cooperative basis. Only cooperatives can support the expanding economic requirements of agriculture, like creating ponds, purchasing machinery, uniting local people to pressure the government for irrigation facilities, etc.

**Boundary plants** – To ensure the maximum utilization of every inch of land, all boundary areas of farms, orchards, schools, homes, etc. should be planted. Trees with spreading branches like most fruit trees and even some timber trees should be planted all around the perimeter at fixed distances. Fruit trees can store large amounts of water in their roots, so they should be planted around the fields to help conserve water. The water stored in the roots of the trees will keep the soil moist and fertile.

Then palm trees, which grow straight up, should be planted in between the fruit trees, as they do not block the sunlight from the fruit trees. Between the fruit trees and the palm trees, filler plants should be planted, such as edible anthurium (*mankaccu*) or arrowroot, both root crops. Sisal makes a good fill-up plant in boundaries; it helps to prevent erosion.

In between all these, there is still space for associate plants to the filler plants; with anthurium, plant Chinese minigrape; with arrowroot, plant mini bitter gourd. Where trees are planted around the boundary of agricultural land, care should be taken so that they do not shade the crops from the sun.

The boundary wall should also be planted with fruit, flower and vegetable creepers. Creepers such as bougainvilleas, madhurilata, etc. should be planted above the entrance gate at compounds.

### My master on new concept of political science:

Benefits of a World Government– Some benefits of a world government are 1. The huge expense of maintaining a military in each country will be saved and these savings can be used by people for our benefit. 2. It will reduce psychic tension. 3. There will be less bloodshed due to warfare, 4. There would be freer movement of people.

A world government will also allow us to nurture and develop a universal value system. For the existence of human society at this critical time in history, unit social security and peace are fundamental needs. We will not be able to survive without them. A world government is essential to make these a reality. This government will allow us to develop a common philosophy of life, the same constitutional structure, a common penal code and the availability of the minimum essentialities of life. It will also provide us with a world militia, which will fight for the welfare of humanity as a whole.

Human Sentiments Predominate - Human beings are predominantly sentimental by nature. If the sentiment for a particular idea is adjusted with the collective sentiment, then the sentiment can be utilized for establishing unity in the human society. Sometimes the human sentiment for an idea runs counter to the collective sentiment and as such creates greater disunity. Therefore those sentiments, which are conducive to human unity, should be encouraged, and those that create a rift in human society should be rejected. Within nation states today, people are moved by many sentiments. For example, they love their own language and they love to hear about the glorious history of their country. Politicians, however, do not utilize these sentiments for the further advancement of humanity, rather they use them only to identify themselves with the status quo and there by maintain their present power base.

The unity that grows from the collective philosophy in the social, psychic and economic spheres is the first step towards a greater unity. This can lead to the formation of a nation or greater internal unity in a country. But once the problem out of which the sentiment was born is no longer the sentiment fades. Therefore, for unity to be continued, a spiritual outlook is necessary. To establish lasting unity in human society, the spiritual sentiment is indispensable. Every human being has a spiritual thirst. Knowingly or unknowingly, human beings are searching for the Supreme Being.

The Need for a Universal Ideology and Spiritual Practices - A universal ideology, which is not confined to time, space, and person but based on the Absolute, is necessary to unite humanity. When the limited mind accepts the unlimited entity as its objects, the mind goes on expanding to a full 360 degrees. The method that brings about psycho-spiritual progress is called spiritual practice. When human beings bring the entire universe within the range of their minds through spiritual practice, the result will be one universal society. As long as the feeling of nationalism remains alive, mutual conflicts are inevitable. Human welfare depends on the degree of psychic expansion. When nationalism cannot embrace every human being, that nation cannot attain perfect well being. When the welfare of some individual's remains outside the scope of the limited mind of the nationalists, their sorrows will never is felt. That is why a group of nationalists may attack another group of nationalists just to establish their national ego. Not even internationalism is able to attain the highest degree of mental expansion. Who can say that human civilization does not exist on other planets? Indeed, in spite of government interference, there is much proof of this already. Such a thought remains outside the minds of those who think only about national and international relations. It is not possible for internationalists to establish universalism. The only way to establish universalism is to bring about mental expansion through spiritual practices.

Spiritual values can lead to the establishment of a universal state, a global nation. This nation would truly be a "Human Nation."

The Determinants of a Nation (State) - What are the determinants of a Nation (State) and what is the potential for humanity to unite under a spiritual ideology?

The status of a nation depends on the following factors: 1. Common history, 2. Common tradition, 3. Common territory, 4. Common race, 5. Common faith, 6. Common language and 7. Common ideals. The stronger these factors the stronger the national identity.

Cosmic Ownership - Among these factors, items one to seven are relative and therefore transitory. Only within the last item is there scope for a blending of the Absolute with relative truth. The absolute factor here would be the ideal Cosmic Ownership. The Universe is the creation of the cosmic entity and as such its ownership is with the cosmic entity. We may enjoy and utilize the objects and have stewardship over them but we cannot claim ownership over them. This factor needs to be accepted within the scope of political thought. Non-governmental organizations of the world are positioned to take the lead in helping to manifest this value within human society.

Intellectual Revolution - It will take an intellectual revolution to realize this great ideal. Irrational dogmas will have to be rejected. The NGOs that are now fighting to save the planet are also able to take the lead in creating this spiritual ideal. Intellectual revolution has a democratic nature. Those people who grasp the spiritual ideals as their own can popularize the idea among the intellectual community. People can be organized to create these universal values. We can support moral leadership in democratic contests at all levels and especially in the gaining of seats in the proposed second Assembly of the United Nations.

Collective Psychology - What is the relationship between leadership and morality? PROUT recognizes that within the collective psychology of human society, there are four basic psychologies. These are rooted in the ability to have control over the psycho-physical parallelism of relative reality. There is 1. The labour psychology, which is embodied by the masses or people who are unable to gain power over the flow of forces. They are always laboring at the mercy of external forces and when contentment comes, it is only in being able to meet their basic necessities and satisfy basic needs. 2. The warrior psychology embodied by the military and athletes who are able to gain power by virtue of physical force. 3. The intellectual psychology embodied by the educators, priests, artists and others who are able to gain power by virtue of mental force. And 4. The merchant psychology embodied by the business people who are able to have power by virtue of their control over money, the universal medium of exchange.

If we look at any period of history, or if we examine the social forces in the contemporary society, we can see the activity of the groups and classes who embody these basic psychologies. Historians divide western history into ancient, medieval and modern history because the activity of each age is so distinct from its predecessor. This is because the dominant psychology of each age was different. In ancient times the warrior kings established their empires. In the middle Ages, the intellectuals ruled through the Catholic Church. And in modern times, those with a business mentality rule through the vehicle of multinational corporations.

It is a tenet of PROUT that there is a social code in which the warrior age gives way to the intellectual age and the intellectual to the merchant in an endless cycle. We are nearing the end of the first collective social cycle. Therefore, PROUT predicts that the present merchant age will give way to a warrior age that will initiate the second round of the cycle.

Social Cycle - PROUT also recognizes a fifth psychology

that is embodied by the spiritualists and moralists who are able to gain power by virtue of their love of the people and their love of the Supreme Entity. In history, the moralists were always few and far between. Through Neo-Humanism in the present day, such class-consciousness is emerging and will continue to emerge. Bonds are developing between those who seek unity with the cosmic entity and who are willing to offer their lives in selfless service to suffering humanity. It is the task of such moralists and spiritualists to lead the struggle to form a World Government, to avert cataclysmic destruction and move the planet into peace and harmony. In Prout theory, such moralists are called *Sadvipras*. *Sadvipras* are actually a declassed group because they do not represent the interests of any group but of humanity as a whole. To achieve this 360 degree expansion, they must develop within themselves the abilities of each of the other psychologies. They must be accomplished in all ways of thinking and acting.

**The Role of Sadvipras (Moral Leaders)**—Theoretically, Sadvipras may or may not assume public offices but they would always be involved in developing social policies that are beneficial to the whole of humanity. They would be particularly active during periods of transition in the social cycle.

Under such conditions it would be their task to ensure that such transitions occur with a minimum of social destruction. In the dialectical movement of society each psychology comes into dominance among the masses of people because it brings benefits to them. Those who embody the consciousness of the age become its new leaders. The ideas of these leaders become the ruling ideas of the age. As the new class of leaders strives to create a new social system, they establish a status quo. This is the period of greatest prosperity for all. Yet, as life moves on and conditions change, the ruling class attempts to maintain the status quo even in the face of necessary change. As time passes they become an oppressive force. Because they do not have a spiritual perspective, they seek only their own interests at the expense of society as a whole. This was true for the Roman nobility, it was true of the Catholic Church in the middle ages, and it is true today of the owners of capitalists who stand in the way of environmental and social progress in order to protect and foster their own class interests.

Under present conditions it is the task of all moral people to unite and create the conditions of social change and lead people into the new age. The vehicle for this transition will be a world government that is able to bring local representation into a global political forum.

### True Leadership

Can a World Government be established without staging a fight? Yes in theory. The survival of human society and the planet can be achieved by mobilizing those people who are desirous of establishing the world federation, not simply by political means, but by the means of selfless service and constructive work. Our leaders will have to remain engaged in the task of social welfare with all sincerity and without motive. People who render this service will be the best possible to establish the world federation. If government leaders throw themselves behind such a mission it should be a happy day for humanity. Where governments will not associate themselves with this task, the people will become increasingly agitated in their suffering and, they, under the leadership of the *sadvipras*, will have to establish the world government. Can blind physical force or intellectual theorizing establish the role of the sadvipras? No, a moral leadership can come about only through the systematic and rational application of universal principles by many intelligent people. Therefore, it is necessary for the leaders of a universal society to reveal the present predicament of society to the people so they can understand the truth. They should make an all out effort to raise people's consciousness at

the earliest.

Does the rule of sadvipras (moral leaders) mean that they will hold the highest offices of the world government? No, not necessarily. Sadvipras will rule when the people value their contributions to society regardless of their social or political position. The position of sadvipras in the actual political structure will be better understood as we explore the structure of the world government as envisioned in Prout.

World Government can be created step by step—Prout sees the world government evolving through stages. Initially, corporate and national interests and people's fears will hinder the formation of a world federation. Therefore, the work of creating a world government must be carried on step by step to remove the baseless fears. In addition, due consideration will have to be paid to remove its inherent difficulties. The world government will have to be taken to the highest level by strengthening it slowly and not suddenly. In the first stage the world government should act only as a legislative body. It can be invested with the framing rights of implementation or non-implementation of any particular law in any particular region. Its initial task should be to create a world constitution. Given present circumstances, it is necessary that a legislature be operating by the year 2000 at the latest.

A Legislative Body at the Global Level—The Legislature should consist of two councils. The lower council should be comprised of representatives from the different parts of the world, elected on the basis of population, who advocate the interests of humanity as a whole. The upper council members will be chosen by nation states.

The resolution of the people will be introduced through the lower house, where it will be discussed and submitted to the upper house for final approval.

Given present conditions, the general assembly of the United Nations could possibly be converted to form the upper council of the Legislature, while the Second Assembly, advocated by NGOs, could become the lower council.

Under this arrangement, national governments would maintain administrative power, but they would not have the power to make laws. Such an arrangement would make it difficult for any national government to oppress political minorities or destroy the earth according to the whims of governing majorities or petty tyrants.

In following stages, the World Government can be progressively strengthened to include Executive and Judiciary branches. These three branches of government would provide a system of checks and balances against the abuse of power as in the present Westminster system or the USA system.

Prout envisions present trends leading to the complete downfall of communist thought which will leave no lasting impression on humanity because it denies individual initiative and the spiritual nature of life. Further, Prout envisions capitalism continuing its decline. In its constriction, it will exclude more and more people as it continues largely unabated to destroy the natural environment. As this trend continues capitalism will also be destroyed and the world will move into a martial age. The attempts of human populations to gain equilibrium out of the fall of capitalism and communism will result in the formation of a world government, which in its initial phase will be a legislative body. This is the likely scenario.

The Problem with Democracy—Although the fall of communist governments in many countries has prompted a more universal desire for democracy, Prout does not wholeheartedly support the western notion of democracy as simply adult franchise. In all truthfulness, the right of people to vote, on acquiring a certain age, has some fundamental defects. For example, the qualities of a candidate cannot be judged by the majority unless the majority itself has acquired a fair level of consciousness. Even in developed countries people are

increasingly reluctant and uninterested in finding true leaders. This attitude exists largely because people cannot find true leaders from the political parties in power. Education, under such conditions, provides no cure either, because in the modern capitalist society, education only means a collection of pieces of information and training in some economic skills. It does not arouse maturity, conscience or social consciousness. These virtues neither come through age, or status, or instruction. For this, human beings have to realize their own nature. We need to pursue a course of spiritual self-development along with physical and mental development and observe a code of universal morality.

Of all present government structures, democracy is the most appreciated. It is defined as government of the people, for the people and by the people. But in fact, it is really the rule of the majority, and the psychology of the majority is determined by the ruling class values because the ruling class has the power to communicate its values over the popular media and run candidates for office.

Even in a democracy like the United States, the government may be very keen on passing laws to check corruption and social degeneration but the government is not equally keen on enforcing the laws because the political leaders must protect the rich and powerful whose interests usually run contrary to the needs of the majority. Look at the crises in the social services and the education system in the United States and you will see this. The majority of the society is fooled. The wise people are always in a minority.

**Factors for Democracy to be Successful**—In order for Democracy to be successful, three important factors are necessary. These are: 1. Literacy, 2. Morality and 3. Social, economic, and political consciousness. Yet even if these three requirements are met, democracy still does not rationally offer the best system for the real welfare of society. Those political parties who teach literacy can spread their respective dogmas and undermine the rational judgment and discrimination of the people. So also morality is easily corrupted by democracy. Majority votes are often used by antisocial elements against moral minorities. Finally, regarding social, economic and political consciousness, even educated people can be misguided by shrewd and cunning politicians.

Because of the above reasons PROUT believes that the present definition of democracy as universal suffrage is unable to ensure the welfare of society.

**Benevolent Rule by Sadvipras** - The best solution to human governance, theoretically, is an enlightened and benevolent rule by the Sadvipras, something akin to a council of elders. This is because sadvipras value human life above all other values. Be it state or scripture, society or religion, the significance of everything lies in developing humanity to the optimum point through knowledge, culture, health and affluence of life. Yet, obviously, such a form of government cannot be immediately achieved. Sadvipras need to be developed. A process needs to be set in motion to create them. Nonetheless, in the interim, the idea of democracy must be merged with the idea of moral leadership. From purely a political perspective, Prout suggests that the NGO movements should influence the Brazil 92 conference, and to create a world government, holds out the greatest promise to create a world government led by moralists. To accomplish this, Prout suggests that these movements, as a first step, begin to establish social boards as a part of their decision-making process.

**The Social Boards**—What is a social board? A social board is a new development in the history of political structures. Its purpose is to act as a check and balance to the formal government structure. This is necessary because in any era, the government tends to favor those who express the dominant psychology.

The Social Boards or *Sadvipra* Boards should be composed of members who are engaged in social welfare activities like education, the arts, the sciences, social services and community projects. These people already enjoy popular confidence because they are continually providing social services and fighting bureaucratic inefficiency and corruption in society. By virtue of their social service and universal values, the social boards will form a dispersed nucleus throughout society and form a check against government corruption and manipulation of the people. Should any government go against the needs of the people, the social boards will organize the people and change the government. It is the social board that creates beneficial influence. Therefore, it is the duty of the Proutists to help Sadvipras become powerful and to strengthen their hand among people.

As a first step, a Global Society Board should be established. The Global society board will formulate board policy objectives that will be guidelines for government legislation. The first Global social board should be a committee of these NGO representatives who are true leaders and who, through selfless service, have gained the respect of the people.

Subsequent tasks of the board are

1. Select members of social boards at the national level below theirs,
2. Select candidates for government office at their level,
3. Develop social policy as per the needs of the people as a whole,
4. Select voters to the electoral colleges at its level.

In time, social boards will exist at all levels: state, national and global. At that time, the global social board should also select its chair persons who will be the constitutional head of the world government, the social boards and the electoral colleges. They will form a stable triangle of forces.

After a period of transition, social board should be elected by electoral colleges at their level.

Electoral colleges—What are Electoral Colleges? The Electoral Colleges are PROUT's answer to defects in universal adult suffrage. They ensure adult suffrage for those committed to social welfare only. The Electoral colleges would be open to any potential voter who can demonstrate that he or she has a developed social, economic and political consciousness and moral integrity.

Membership in the Electoral College would be open to all those who demonstrate their awareness and integrity by taking a practical test, in much the same way a person qualifies for a driver's license. In the initial stage, the Global Social Board would provide guidelines for such a test. These could be modified at the lower levels but they must remain consistent with cardinal human values. In this way, we can say that the social board initially selects the electoral colleges and later is elected by it.

Voting would be compulsory for members of the Electoral College. Electoral Colleges should eventually exist at the local, state, national and global levels.

The functions of the Electoral College eventually would be to:

1. Elect the best candidates to government at its level,
2. Elect members to the social board at its level,
3. Elect members from the Electoral College to the next highest level.

The Electoral College would eventually elect all members of the legislature and the judiciary at its level. It would not, however, elect any members of the executive branch. Here, the social board and he would select the chief executive officer would appoint the cabinet.

Government - What should be the structure of the world government? There should be four layers of government; a world federation, national governments, state governments and

local self governments.

The World Federation would function as a coordinating body. It would also have three branches. The Executive, Legislative and Judiciary, in the initial stage only the legislative branch will be active. This branch will frame the constitution. Its members will derive a process of transition from the present order to the new order.

In the future, the social board would select candidates, for office at any level. From the selected candidates the Electoral College would elect members of the Legislative and Judiciary branches. The Social Board would appoint the candidate for the chief office of the Executive. This officer would then choose his or her cabinet. The Electoral College would not vote on any positions in the Executive branch.

The candidate who has a majority of the votes (51% or more) would be elected to office. If more than two people run for the same office and each receives less than 51%, there would be a run off election between the two candidates with the highest votes.

All elected candidates to the public office should have to publish a signed manifesto prior to the date of their elections and be held liable to judicial prosecution if they violate their published manifesto. Prout calls this system compartmentalized Democracy or the Selecto-Electoral system.

**World Constitution** - The Formation of a World Government will require a World Constitution. Historically, the dominating classes framed laws in different eras of their domination. The protagonists of class interests have always neglected human values. Discarding the narrow considerations of religion and class there should be one universally accepted law. A charter or principles or bill of rights should be included in such a constitution and encompass at least the following four areas:

1. Complete security should be guaranteed to all species of plants and animals on the planet,
2. Each country must guarantee purchasing power to meet the basic necessities of all its citizens,
3. The constitution should guarantee four fundamental rights: spiritual practice, cultural legacy, education and indigenous linguistic expression,
4. If the practice of any of these rights conflicts with cardinal human values then that practice should be immediately curtailed. Cardinal human values must take precedence over all other rights.

**Cardinal Human Values** - What are cardinal Human values? In Prout theory, human existence is trifarious: physical, mental, and spiritual. Most people, due to survival struggles, cannot transcend the limits of their physical existence. Worldly pleasures become the only enjoyment of their lives. Goaded and tormented by physical desires, the subtle expressions and practices of life are beyond their reach.

Other people are more concerned with their minds. They feel that it is the supremacy of the mind that has differentiated them from animals. Their lives are guided by their search for mental satisfaction.

Still others believe that the mind flows for the sole purpose of attaining the infinite, and they focus their energies on the contemplation of the cosmic entity. These are the spiritual aspirants.

All human beings can reach this confluence of the mind and spiritual strata. Just as the culminating point of animality is the beginning of humanity, so too the highest peak of humanity is the beginning of divinity. The meeting point of the highest attainment of humanity and the blossoming of divinity is the base on which the cardinal human values are established. Herein, the significance of everything is to develop humanity to the optimum.

History reveals that nowhere have cardinal human values

been truly honored. The warriors, on gaining power, engaged in pursuit of pleasure and comforts, utterly neglecting their sacred duty to serve the subjects. They never cared to know people's suffering. For self-aggrandizement and in a bid to conquer the world, they invaded one country after another. How could they afford to inquire into the tragic plight of the common people?

The intellectual era demonstrates the same thing. They were not accessible to the common people. All their energies were spent in appeasement of the gods and goddesses enthroned in the temples, churches, or mosques. These structures of brick and wood were more important than humanity itself. Ensnared in scripture, formulated to serve vested interests, they did not attend to the value of human life.

In the present age of the business class, only those who possess wealth are respected and revered. Human values have become meaningless because human beings have become the means for the rich to earn money. Those who are at the helm of society are constantly suspicious of others. They are engrossed in counting their profits and losses. They have no desire to think about the plight of humanity.

Therefore, we see that it is customary to prefer social values to human values. Moral leaders will have to address this as a priority.

> "The value of human life exceeds all other values. Be it state or scripture, society or religion, the significance of everything lies in developing humanity to its optimum."

If people are to achieve their highest potential, they need to have an environment suitable to their physical, mental and spiritual existence. It is the birthright of everybody to make headway in his or her trifarious existence. It is the duty of society to accord recognition to this right of humanity. The society has failed to do this and that is why life is full of sorrows and sufferings.

Every course of action of society ought to be judged with an eye to the dictum "Human beings are divine children."

**Common Philosophy of Life** - It is necessary that cardinal human values provide the basis of our common ideology. People need to unite under a common idea. Unless this is achieved, there is little chance of social synthesis. Such ideology needs to be based on the absolute and not the relative. The absolute ideal should not only be the culminating point but also the starting point from which we will always be supplied with vital energy. We want a cosmic society, a cosmic tie with all of life. For the sake of this unity, we should encourage our common needs and discourage all fissiparous tendencies.

**Universal Penal Code** - A universal penal code should also be prepared on the basis of a constitutional structure. The whole concept of vice and virtue needs to be analyzed. The difference between natural law, moral law, and human law should be minimized. Correction, not punishment, should be the guiding purpose of the penal code.

**Decentralized Economic Democracy** - Most social alienation and crime today is based on the inability of people to meet their minimum necessities, Prout advocates that the world government should be instrumental in establishing an economic system which guarantees the availability of the minimum necessities of life. This is one of the fundamental principles of PROUT's economic theory. Food, shelter, clothing, education and medicine constitute the minimum of life. Maximum amenities can still be given to the people of higher qualities but only after guaranteeing the minimum requirements every person and ensuring ecological balance. To implement a PROUT economy, the world government should help local people from autonomous units, not necessarily present states, with which they can easily identify and which should look after the problems of the local people. The boundaries of these units need to be readjusted to suit any change in the people's growth, especially the development in the technique of communications

to allow bigger areas to work smoothly and efficiently. When forming such socio-economic units (*samaj*) the following factors should be taken into account: the unit should have similar geographical features, the same economic problems the same potential, ethnic similarity and a common sentimental legacy. Prout advocates decentralized economic democracy guided by a centralized political system. Without economic democracy political democracy is a farce.

**A World Militia**— A world militia is essential to protect the interests of humanity as a whole and to uphold the world constitution. There will always be those factions who will seek to impose their own needs on others by the use of physical force; therefore, the military will always be an indispensable part of society. Militias will also continue to exist at the local, state and national levels.

**One Recognized Language**— One internationally recognized language will be very beneficial in conducting human affairs. This will allow human beings to communicate across all established cultures and geographic boundaries. At this time, the English language seems best suited for the task, although in the future this may change.

# X

## MY MASTER AND THE COURT

> “Build an ideal human society based on dharma. Let this be the sole mission of your life. Perfection is a God-ward movement. Do not be afraid - move on. Obstacles will go down the drain where they belong.”

### The Laws with Controversy

> "A lie goes around the world before the truth gets its britches on"
> — *George Hortzog*

*Life is a game*, He played it.

> "When the lion is in the cage, even a baby can throw stones on him"
> — *Author*

#### 1. Why the Indian Government was against Ananda Marga?

Even before *Ananda Marga* was established, *P. R. Sarkar* was a controversial figure amongst the highest Indian officials in the post-independence days.

In 1947, the Muslims and Hindu leaders decided to divide the country into Pakistan and India. Those areas where the Muslims were in the majority would become Pakistan, whereas the areas with a non-Muslim majority would remain in India. When the newspapers published this, many Muslims felt insecure and started migrating to Pakistan and vice versa, Hindus fled to India.

There is a district in East Pakistan (now Bangladesh) called *Khulna* with a Buddhist community as the majority and only a small percentage of Muslims. Even so, this land was given to East Pakistan. This foul-up of the Indian Government went unnoticed until *P. R. Sarkar* wrote a letter to *Shyama Prasad Mukherji*, leader of the Nationalistic *Hindu Maha Sabha*. The letter accused the leaders of carelessness regarding *Khulna's* loss. Mr. *Mukherji*, who received the letter, brought the issue up for parliamentary discussion. Prime Minister *Jawaharlal Nehru*,

after realizing his mistake asked why he raised the matter for discussion so late. Then *Mr. Mukherji* told him about the letter and Nehru demanded it from him.

After independence *Mr. Mallik*, the ex-chief director of the Federal Bureau of Intelligence (F.B.I. now called the C.B.I) published his autobiography. He wrote that after independence, Nehru told him to constantly monitor three parties: 1. *Rastriya Svam sevak Party* 2. *Jinnah's Muslim League* and 3. *P. R. Sarkar*. *Nehru* regarded them as hostile elements against the government.

*Vimalamitra*, a friend of my master, told us that one day he was searching for some files in the office. He discovered a confidential file that contained 45 letters from the government concerning *P. R. Sarkar*. At that time, there were agents and spies from the Criminal Investigation Department working in the railways workshop where Baba and *Vimalamitra* were employed. By these letters, it was obvious that the government was trying its best to find evidence of wrong doing by Baba so they could persecute him. Their attempt failed due to proper reasoning and prejudicial evidence. Meanwhile, the spies continued to gather more information about *Mr. P. R. Sarkar*.

#### 2. Why would Nehru's Government be so antagonized by P. R. Sarkar?

It was well known that *P. R. Sarkar* was the nephew of the great revolutionary *Subhash Chandra Bose*. Bose was His maternal uncle. History shows that the revolutionary zeal of *Subhash* ("strike while the iron is hot") was a complete antithesis to *Gandhi's* passive ideology which "neither breaks the stick nor kills the snake"). As *Nehru* was a disciple of *Gandhi*, he consented to the British colonial demand, as per the rules of agreement, for India's independence, *Nehru* was to capture *Subhash* and hand him over to the British if he returned to India after independence. So naturally, Nehru had adverse feelings against *P. R. Sarkar* when he learned that Baba was the nephew of *Subhash*. The government's hostility grew stronger because sometimes Baba, a mere accountant, used to give advice, offer solutions to the grave problems of India, and rebuke the officials as if He was more capable than them.

3. Once, during the 1960's, Baba told His G.S. (general secy.) to write a letter to the government offering a solution to the beggar problem. G.S. was not convinced with this because there were four million beggars in India at that time. At that time, Ananda Marga was very poor and had a hard time maintaining its fourteen workers. Later, Baba asked if he had sent the letter and he admitted that he had not, he then asked Baba to convince him of the solution first. Baba explained that there are four types of beggars in India:

1. Professional beggars, 2. Kidnappers—who would be purposely deformed by the gangs and then forced to beg, 3. Persons who are lame and disabled through natural calamities, and 4. Orphans.

Baba said that if the government bans begging, then the first category of beggar would be forced through circumstantial pressure to find some other means for their livelihood. The second and third categories would have to be employed according to their ability in innovative cottage industries.
